SECTION 1                                                                                                   6103-E P-1
                                                                  SECTION 1 TURNING CUT FUNCTION

           TURNING CUT FUNCTION

1-1. Overview

            The turning cut function performs cutting on a machining center by synchronizing the circular motion
            of the feed axis with the spindle's turning angle (tool tip position control).

            (1) The tool turns by synchronizing high-speed circular motion on the feed axes X and Y and the
                   turning angle of the spindle, and the position of the turning tool tip is synchronously controlled
                   so that it is kept perpendicular to the workpiece face.

            (2) The cross section configuration is commanded with Z-X as in a turning program, and the X-axis
                   is commanded with the radius of X-Y continuous circular motion.

            The above features enable you to turn cylindrical workpieces and inside/outside diameter tapers on
            the machining center.

                                                                                                                                                                                 ME32123R0700300010001

           Fig.1-1 Image example of turning cut on inside diameter taper
                                                                            6103-E P-2
                                   SECTION 1 TURNING CUT FUNCTION

1-2. Turning Cut Function Program

(1) The program can be commanded in the very same manner as turning on a lathe according to a
      turning program (Z-X).
      With the center of the axes (0, 0) as the origin of the workpiece coordinate system:

        continuous circular motion can be commanded in place of the spindle; and

        the feed axis dimension (Z) and the radius (X) cab be commanded.

        ME32123R0700300020001

(2) Command list
      The table below shows a list of commands for turning cut:

G code                                    Description of command
 G148
 G149   Turns OFF the turning cut command. (Spindle position control OFF)

 G164   Turns ON the turning cut command. (Spindle position control ON)
 G165   This G code brings the machine into turning cut mode.
 G166   With G165 (G166) command, the spindle is indexed at a certain angle from
        the positive X-axis direction.

        The command for turning cut continuous circular motion stop.

        The command for turning cut continuous circular motion start CW (clockwise).

        The command for turning cut continuous circular motion start CCW
        (counterclockwise).

The mode and the cycle are distinguished as shown below:

                                                                 ME32123R0700300020002
(3) Commanding method                                                                                6103-E P-3
       - G148                                              SECTION 1 TURNING CUT FUNCTION
       - G149 [E_]
                          : Turns OFF the turning cut function and spindle position control.

                          : Turns ON the turning cut function and the spindle position
                               control. The spindle position is controlled so that the spindle is
                               directed to the X-Y travel path direction.
                               Specify an E command to direct the spindle to the positive X
                               direction (initial spindle direction 0°).
                               The NC controls the spindle angle so that the tool tip is indexed
                               at a certain angle from the positive X-axis direction.

                       E : Designation of C-axis offset value
                               Specify the C-axis offset value for each of the tools.
                               Command range: 0.000 to 359.999°
                               (If the command value is larger than 360°, it is rounded to an
                               angle within 360°)
                               If the E command is omitted, the data set at the NC optional
                               parameter (long word) No. 29 is used instead.

[Supplement]

 Set the C-axis offset amount when the positive-direction movement of the X-axis is 0 degrees
 with NC optional parameter long word No. 29. Set this value in the minimum unit according to the
 VAC specification code.

- G164                    : Stops continuous circular motion commanded by G165 (G166)
- G165 X_ [Z_] S_              on the start axis X.

                          : Clockwise continuous circular motion is performed on the X-axis
                               and the Y-axis with the distance from the starting point to the
                               center of the workpiece origin set as the radius.

                       X : Alarm B occurs if there is no commanded X-axis command with
                               the start position for continuous circular motion set as the radius.

                       Z : No alarm occurs even if there is no commanded Z-axis
                               command for the start position for continuous circular motion.

                       S : The commanded (rpm (= min-1)) S command value becomes
                               valid with the feedrate of continuous circular motion set in speed
                               only when G165 is commanded simultaneously.

[Supplement]

 Alarm B occurs if the selected plane is not the Z-X plane (G18), the current position is not on the
 X-axis (Y  0) when commanded, or there is no S command in the same block.
 Alarm B also occurs if a command for an axis other than the X- or Z- axis is set. A one
 micrometer command cannot be given.
 Any value, which cannot be divided in the minimum command unit of micron, will be rounded off.
 When the command value is X133 m

     The continuous circular motion radius is X66 m.
 When the command value is X133 mm

     The continuous circular motion radius is X66.5 mm.  X66500 m

- G166 X_ [Z_] S_      : Counterclockwise continuous circular motion is performed on
                           the X-axis and the Y-axis with the distance from the starting
                           point to the center of the workpiece origin set as the radius.
                           Other contents are the same as those of G165.

                                   (Refer to the instruction manual for nose radius compensation
                                   function.)

                    S50 ...... Starts the turning cut cycle (CW).

                     Positioning to continuous circular motion start position

                       X50Z0

G01 Z-50 F50                  Beginning continuous circular motion of 50 mm in radius at

         X80                     50 turns per minute

         X70 Z-110  ...... Cutting to -50 mm in the Z-axis direction at a feedrate of 50 mm
                           per minute.

                    ...... Positioning to the next hole machining position.

G188
G148                ...... Cutting to ­110 mm in the Z-axis direction at a feedrate of 50
M02                        mm per minute, and synchronously reducing the radius of
                           continuous circular motion to 35 mm.

                    ...... Terminates the turning cut cycle and stops continuous circular
                           motion on X-axis.

                    ...... Nose radius compensation OFF

                    ...... Second tool length offset OFF

                    ...... Turns OFF the turning cut mode and the spindle position control.

                   Positioning to the start position (Y0 point in positive X direction)

G188                     motion on X-axis.

                  ...... Second tool length offset OFF

[Supplement]

 1) The sign of X-axis coordinate value determines the X-axis position from which unlimited
      circular interpolation is started (by G165 or G166).

        If G165X100S50 is specified,

           The machine starts unlimited circular interpolation from the position (X, Y) = (50, 0).

        If G165X­100S50 is specified,

           The machine starts unlimited circular interpolation from the position (X, Y) = (­50, 0).
 2) Notes related with X-axis command in the shape command

      a) The sign of X-axis coordinate value determines the X-axis end position in the shape
          command.
          - If G01X50 is specified,
           This block ends at the position (X, Y) = (25, 0).
          - If G01X­50 is specified,
           This block ends at the position (X, Y) = (­25, 0).

      b) When specifying a negative value in the shape command, use utmost care to prevent the
          spindle from interfering or colliding with the workpiece.

(5) Precautions

        Turn ON nose radius compensation after turning cut function ON (G149) and Z-X plane

            specification (G18) have been executed.

        Turn OFF nose radius compensation before turning cut function OFF (G148) is executed.

        Turn ON second tool length offset before the continuous circular motion start commands

            (G165, G166).

        Suspension and single block are invalid for continuous circular motion. It slows down and

            stops upon resetting.
            Thus, the spindle does not always stop on the X-axis.

        Suspension and single block are invalid for shape commands.

        The F command (feed per revolution) included in the shape command can be changed

            during cycle operation.

        Override (cutting feed/spindle) is invalid for continuous circular motion.

            The commanded speed is 100%.
            The feedrate override is effective in execution of the shape command during the turning cut
            cycle in the feed-per-minute mode but not effective during the turning cut cycle in the feed-
            per-revolution mode.

        Manual interrupt is invalid during a turning cut mode.

        Alarm B occurs if turning cut function ON (G149) is executed during G95 (feed per

            revolution command). Alarm B also occurs if the G95 command is executed in turning cut
            mode.

        Alarm B occurs if sequence return is executed in turning cut mode.

        A sequence number search is prohibited in turning cut mode (because whether turning cut

            mode is selected cannot be identified).
                                                                                                              6103-E P-7
                                                                    SECTION 1 TURNING CUT FUNCTION

 Alarm B occurs if any of the commands listed below is executed in turning cut mode:
      Fixed cycle command
      Area machining
      Coordinate calculation
      Workpiece coordinate system-related command (G15, G16, G92)
      G30 (home position)
      Tool replacement M code

 Alarm B occurs if any of the commands listed below, which are prohibited during a turning

   cut cycle, is executed:

      G11 (parallel/rotational movement of figure)
      Copy function
      G51 (scale-up/scale-down of figure)
      G62 (programmable mirror image)
      Mirror image SW
      G69 (slope coordinate ON)
 Alarm B occurs if any of the following commands listed below is issued during a turning cut

   cycle:

      Y-axis command
      Command other than G01, G02, and G03
      AG command
      Command with which M00 (program stop) or M01 (optional stop) is valid
 No alarm occurs even if the G149 command is issued during machine locking.
 Alarm B occurs if the G149 command is issued when the STM lock function is ON.
 The continuous circular motion angular speed is limited if the feedrate exceeds the specific

   rapid feed speed of the machine by increasing the continuous circular motion radius during
   a turning cut cycle.
   Alarm D remains displayed while the angular speed is limited.

      Turning Cut Actual Position Screen

      The turning cut actual position screen displays the configuration of the cross section during a turning
      cut cycle.
      This screen appears by pressing the page key with the actual position screen displayed.
      During turning cut cycle, the TURNING CUT CYCLE lamp lights in yellow at the upper right of the
      screen.
      The X-axis actual position during a turning cut cycle is displayed in radius.

      S   Circular motion speed on X-axis and Y-axis

      Co  Selected workpiece coordinate number

      H   Tool length offset number

      Hx  Second tool length offset number

      D   Nose radius compensation number (tool radius compensation number)

      [Supplement]

       The S command value displayed on other actual position screens or in block data is the value
       before turning cut mode is selected.
       The S command value displayed remains unchanged even after a turning cut cycle has been
       selected.
                                                                           6103-E P-9
                                  SECTION 1 TURNING CUT FUNCTION

1-4. Pulse Handle Shift Function

If a problem such as tool breakage happens during machining in the turning cut function, a tool can
be retracted without canceling the turning cut function by reset.
Pulse handle override during a turning cut mode (G149 to G148) is valid only in the Z-axis direction.
This is impossible in the X-axis (unlimited circular radius) direction.

                                                                            G149 E_
                                                                            G165 (166) X_ S_

                                During mode During cycle

                                                                            G164
                                                                            G148

                                                                                                                                                   ME32123R0700300040001

Refer to "Pulse Handle Override" for how to operate pulse handle override.

The shift amount is included in the actual position of Z-axis by ticking NC optional parameter bit
No.5 bit 7. The shift amount is not included in the X-axis regardless of this parameter.

[Supplement]

 1) The amount of pulse handle override is "0" when turning on and off the power.
 2) Whether such position data should be reset to "0" when the NC is reset is determined by the

      setting of THE MANUAL SHIFT (ACTUAL/TOTAL) for NC optional parameter (AUTOSET AT
      NC RESET/POWER ON).

4301 Alarm D Turning Cut speed limit (replacing)

        Angular velocity is limited because, in the turning cut cycle, the radius for continuous circular
        movements has exceeded the permissible radius determined by the machine-dependent
        rapid traverse and the angular velocity at that time.

        [Index]
        None

        [Character-string]
        None

        [Code]
        1  Angular velocity for circular movement is limited for the following reason:
        The radius for continuous circular movements has exceeded the allowable radius determined
        by the machine-dependent rapid traverse and the angular velocity at that time.
        2  Angular velocity is limited for the following reason:
        The current acceleration has exceeded the threshold value calculated from servo parameter
        used only for turning cut function.
        3  The target angular velocity is limited for the following reason:
        The target angular velocity determined by the specified speed has exceeded the rotation
        speed limit.

        [Probable Faulty Locations]
        The radius for continuous circular movements is too large.
        The specified rotation speed is too high.

[Supplement]

 In the turning cut, feed axes create rotary motion at high speed according to the radius command
 and rotation speed command specified for circular movements.
 Therefore, if the specified speed is too high for circular movements with a small radius, the
 feedrate command may exceed the maximum acceleration, resulting in DIFF over alarm.
 Moreover, axis movements based on a high-speed command may cause machine vibration and
 torque vibration. Therefore, the NC controls the feedrate by limiting the angular velocity.
SECTION 2                                                                                                 6103-E P-15
                          SECTION 2 SECOND TOOL LENGTH OFFSET FOR TURNING CUT

           SECOND TOOL LENGTH OFFSET FOR
           TURNING CUT

2-1. Overview

            Second tool length offset by the turning cut function is used when the tool length is compensated in
            the bite direction (extrusion amount) separately from the regular compensation in the Z-axis (spindle
            direction).

                                                                                                                                                                          ME32123R0700400010001

Second tool length offset and conventional tool length offset are independent functions. These two
tool length offset functions can be used together.
                                                                                                     6103-E P-16
                      SECTION 2 SECOND TOOL LENGTH OFFSET FOR TURNING CUT

2-2.  Specifications

      Command format

      Second tool length offset ON/OFF command format (G188 and G189 belong to group No. 69.)

      Second tool length offset ON

      G189 P H
                  P; Second tool length offset axis specification

                         Axis address character except the turning axis

                         The X-axis will be specified when no axis is specified, or an alarm will occur if two

                              or more axes are specified.

                         Set the address name on the axis name.

                  H; Second tool length offset number

                         Set compensation amount data with the same compensation number as that of

                              the previous tool length offset.

                         The number of the second tool length offset previously executed will be specified

                              if this number is omitted.

                         The number will be cleared by resetting the NC.

      Second tool length offset OFF

      G188

      Note

      1) When second tool length offset and G15 and G53 - G59 are commanded in the same block, H
          will be considered H of the G code given a higher priority.
          G15 > G53 - G59 > G188, G189

      2) "2253 Alarm B Data word: axis command FFFFFFFF" occurs because of any of the following
          causes when G189 is commanded:

            2 or more axes were specified in second tool length offset axis specification.

              G189 X1Y1H1

            The axis name address data specified for second tool length axis specification was not 1.

              G189 X5 H1

            The turning axis was specified in second tool length offset axis specification.

              G189 A1 H1
      3) Second tool length offset will be canceled with second tool length offset OFF (G188) or NC

          reset (including M02 and M30) power OFF. (The second tool length offset axis will also be
          canceled.) Second tool length offset will not be canceled with G53.
      4) The second tool length offset number will be cleared (H00) with NC reset (including M02, M30)
          power OFF. It will not be cleared with second tool length offset OFF. The H number held will be
          set if H is not specified in the next G189 command.
                                                                                             6103-E P-17
              SECTION 2 SECOND TOOL LENGTH OFFSET FOR TURNING CUT

(3) Program display screen (main, schedule, MDI)
(4) Turning cut actual position screen

                                                         SECTION 3 NOSE RADIUS COMPENSATION

           NOSE RADIUS COMPENSATION

3-1. General Description of Nose Radius

            Turning can be executed with the turning cut mode.
            In this case, the tool tip is given a tip radius, which causes an error between the commanded shape
            and the machined shape. The nose radius compensation function automatically compensates for
            this error caused by this tip radius using a simple program.
            If the hatching portion is machined with the X-axis and the Z-axis as shown below, an error occurs
            as shown in the left figure shown below. The right figure below shows the movement of the tool
            when the nose radius compensation function is used.

                            X-axis

           Z-axis

                   Portion
                   left uncut

When the nose radius compensation  When the nose radius compensation
           function is not used                  function is used

                                                                                             ME32123R0700500010001
3-2.                                                                                                                      6103-E P-21
                                                                          SECTION 3 NOSE RADIUS COMPENSATION

      Nose Radius Compensation and Tool Radius
      Compensation

      The basic operation of nose radius compensation and that of tool radius compensation are the
      same. The nose radius or tool radius will be automatically compensated when the operator
      executes the program according to the shape to be machined. Difference between nose radius
      compensation and tool radius compensation is described below:
      The nose radius compensation compensates for the radius by setting the nose radius center
      position with the offset amount and the position number to the imaginary tool tip.
      In the mean time, the tool radius compensation compensates for the radius by setting the tool radius
      as the offset amount to the cutter center (cutter turning center).

      Nose Radius Compensation Program

      The nose radius compensation program is the very same as the tool radius compensation program.

      (1) Specifying (Selecting) a plane  Xp...X-axis or U-axis
                                          Yp...Y-axis or V-axis
             G17: Xp Yp plane command     Zp...Z-axis or W-axis
             G18: Zp Xp plane command
             G19: Yp Zp plane command

      (2) G codes used for nose radius compensation

      G40:  Canceling nose radius compensation
      G41:
            Left cutting (The center of the tool passes on the left side of the machined face to
      G42:  the relative motion direction of the tool.)

            Right cutting (The center of the tool passes on the right side of the machined face to
            the relative motion direction of the tool.)

                                                                                                                                                                 ME32123R0700500050001

      (3) Command form

             G17 G41 (G42) Xp_ Yp_ D_
             [G18 G41 (G42) Zp_ Xp_ D_]
             [G19 G41 (G42) Yp_ Zp_ D_]
             D: Nose R Compensation number (0 to 999)

                 When DA, DB or DC is used in command, nose R compensation is not performed.
                 (The program command is regarded as P=0.)

        Compensation Mode

or reversing the positive or negative sign of the compensation amount.

G code                               Offset amount sign

  G41                 +                                               -
  G42
                      Offset to left (left side cutting)       Offset to right (right side cutting)

                      Offset to right (right side cutting)     Offset to left (left side cutting)

Execution conditions

Mode    Command       Straight line - Straight line -          Arc -          Arc - arc

 G41        G41       straight line                       arc  straight line
 G42        G42
 G41        G42                                   Invalid
 G42        G41          (when the offset amount sign is not changed)

                         Executed                              An alarm is issued when there
                                                                       is no cross point.

The compensation direction is changeable, irrespective of whether inside cutting or outside cutting
is performed. In the following description, the compensation amount is positive.

3-11. Precautions on Nose Radius Compensation

            (1) Nose radius compensation amount command
                   Command a number for the compensation amount with the D command. Command it in the
                   same block as G41 and G42, but the D command previously commanded will be used when it
                   is not omitted.
                   The D commands that can be set are from D00 to D999 and DA, DC and DC. When D00 is
                   specified, compensation value is regarded as "0". When DA, DC or DC is used, compensation
                   is not performed. (The program command is regarded as P=0.) Set the compensation amount
                   on the TOOL OFFSET/COMPANSATION screen in tool data setting mode.

            (2) Changing the compensation amount
                   When the compensation amount is changed in compensation mode, it will become valid at the
                   end of the block.

                                                                     ME32123R0700500110001

(3) Actual position display
      The imaginary cutter tip is displayed as the actual position.

(4) When cutting the inside of an arc that is smaller than the nose radius
      Alarm stop (in single block mode)

                                                                                                                                                        ME32123R0700500110002

In this case, cutting is impossible, and an alarm stop occurs. However, the tool stops in a block
several blocks ahead of the block when single block mode is not selected, or in the second
block from the block when single block mode is selected.

      This problem occurs when a step smaller than the nose radius is machined.

a. In outside cutting, the corner is cut along a polygonal tool path. The movement mode and
      the feedrate at the corner conform to those commanded in the next block. However, the
      tool moves as if G01 is selected when the next mode is G02 or G03.

                                                                                                                                                                                  ME32123R0700500110004

b. When the movement of the corner is very small as shown in the figure below, the rear
      movement point will be ignored when Vx  V and Vy  V. Set the value of this V
      with NC optional parameter long word No. 4. The initial value is 0.050 mm.

                                                                                                                                                                                        ME32123R0700500110005
                                                                                                                    6103-E P-48
                                                                    SECTION 3 NOSE RADIUS COMPENSATION

      c. This processing does not take place when the next block is forms a full circle.

                                                                                                                                                                                                    ME32123R0700500110006

            In the above figure:
              P0 - P1 - P2Straight line
              P2 - P3Straight line
              From P3Full circle

            However, if the movement from P2 to P3 is neglected in this program in minute movement
            processing:

              P0 - P1 - P2Straight line
              P2 - P3Arc
            The program neglects the full circle will be neglected and forms an arc from P2 to P3.
(7) Interference

Note
Interference refers to excessive cutting of the tool into the workpiece. The NC checks it in advance.
The NC judges that excessive cutting (interference) will occur when:
the difference between the movement direction in the program and the movement direction
resulting from radius compensation is between 90 degrees and 270 degrees.
As a result, cutting may be considered to be interference in this check even if the tool does not
interfere with the workpiece, or interference may not be detected in this check.
When a corner is cut along a polygonal tool path, an interference check is performed at the last
point of the last corner and at the first point of the next corner, assuming that the points are P1, P2,
P3, and P4 and P5, P6, P7, and P8 (up to four points are formed at a corner). If interference is
detected at a point, the point will be ignored and the next points will be checked with each other.
When no interference is detected halfway, no interference check will be performed at the
subsequent points. The movement mode in this case is straight line movement. If the block is an
arc, the tool moves in G01 mode.
If the tool interferes with the workpiece after all the points have been checked, an interference
alarm occurs, and this last point is not ignored. Consequently, over-cutting may be performed in
single block mode.

      Representative examples are given on the pages that follow to describe the above.
                                                                                                              6103-E P-49
                                                              SECTION 3 NOSE RADIUS COMPENSATION

d. Non-interference which is regarded as interference

                                                                                                                                                                ME32123R0700500110010

      Although the tool does not interfere with the workpiece when N4 - N5 is smaller than the
      tool diameter, an interference alarm occurs because the movement direction of N4 - N5 is
      opposite to that of P4 - P5.
e. Minute arc and quasi-full circle
      A minute arc is defined as an arc whose horizontal and vertical distances from the starting
      point to the end point are smaller than NC optional parameter long word No. 9. A quasi-full
      circle is defined as an arc whose horizontal and vertical distances at the break are smaller
      than NC optional parameter long word No. 9.

                                                                                                                                                          ME32123R0700500110011
                                                                                                        6103-E P-51
                                                        SECTION 3 NOSE RADIUS COMPENSATION
When X  V and Y  V, the arcs are considered as shown on the preceding page
(however, V = NC optional parameter long word No. 9).
In the case of these arcs, an interference check is specially treated. Interference may be
detected although the tool does not interfere with the workpiece due to an operational error
inside. With regard to minute arcs and quasi-full circles, an interference, which is detected
in an interference check, is not treated as an interference but as an operational error.
Additionally, the end point of a minute arc is neglected and treated as a point, thus skipping
the circular movement. The end of a quasi-full circle is also neglected, and the circle is
treated as a full circle from the starting point.

                                                                                                                                                                                ME32123R0700500110012

SECTION 4                                                                                                  6103-E P-54
                                              SECTION 4 2-ROTARY TABLE CONTROL FUNCTION

            2-ROTARY TABLE CONTROL FUNCTION

4-1. Overview

            This function controls two rotary tables. The axes are named A and C and mounted on pallets A and
            B of the 2-pallet system respectively. The A- and C-axes are only controlled by program commands
            when residing on the internal pallet. The rotary table on the external pallet can be manually operated
            regardless of the operation mode. This function is also effective when both rotary tables have been
            removed. (For the pallets positioned side by side, pallets A and B should be read as pallets L and
            R.)

4-2. Details of 2-Rotary Table Control Function

4-2-1. Axis Names

          Rotary Axis Parameters

The axes used for this function are initially named A and C.
The rotary axis names used in program commands can be changed using rotary axis parameters.
The axis name of "A" is used for program commands whichever of the A- and C-axis rotary tables is
in the machine.

Example 1:  If the A-axis is designated as "A" and the C-axis as "C" using rotary axis parameters,
Example 2:  "A" is used in program commands for the rotary table on the internal pallet.

            If the A-axis is designated as "C" and the C-axis as "B," "C" is used in program
            commands for the rotary table on the internal pallet.

Note

This manual describes the control function assuming that the axes have not been renamed by
rotary axis parameters.
                                                                                         6103-E P-55
                             SECTION 4 2-ROTARY TABLE CONTROL FUNCTION

Indication of Axis Position

As mentioned in 4-2-1, the name of the A-axis is always used in program commands for the rotary
table on the internal pallet. In order to clarify which axis is to be operated by a program command,
however, the names of the A- and C-axes are indicated on the screen and followed by a smaller
letter, "A" for the axis on the internal pallet or "C" for the one on the external pallet.

                                                                                                                                                                                           ME32123R0700600030001

Note
If the axis on the external pallet is manually operated during execution of a program, the actual
speed includes the moving speed of the axis on the external pallet.
                                                                                                                                 6103-E P-56
                                                                     SECTION 4 2-ROTARY TABLE CONTROL FUNCTION

4-2-2. Internal/External Rotary Tables

          Distinction between Internal and External Rotary Tables

If a program command for the internal axis is issued with both A- and C-axes indicated as external,
"2359 Alarm B Data word: rotating axis command 5" appears.

Initialization of the Axis Relocated from Internal to External Position

If an axis moves from the internal to external position, the manual shift (current/cumulative) for that
axis is cleared.

Initialization of the Axis Relocated from the External to Internal Position

If an axis is manually operated as external to move from the internal to external position, the former
position may change. Therefore, the current position of the axis is re-determined when it moves
from the external to internal position. As a result, the incremental command specifies the position
relative to the current position, not the position relative to the OSP-specific last specified position.

Identification of Internal and External Axes

When it is necessary to identify the internal axis in a part program, use the pallet number (VPLTK)
as a system variable. If this number is 1, pallet A resides at the internal position and, if it is 2, pallet
B resides at the internal position.

4-2-3. Parameters

The NC optional parameter bits related with the rotary axes can be independently set for each axis.

NC optional parameter  Multi-turn rotary table     A-axis        B-axis        C-axis
              bit                               No. 39 bit 3  No. 39 bit 4  No. 39 bit 5

4-2-4. Program Commands

          Basic Items

Program commands for the rotary table should be issued as for the A-axis. If a command for the C-
axis is issued, "2359 Alarm B Data word: rotating axis command 5" appears.

Home Position Change Command

The home position of an external axis does not change with the G-coded home position change
command (G30) or M-coded home position change command (M6 or M60).
                                                                                                                    6103-E P-57
                                                        SECTION 4 2-ROTARY TABLE CONTROL FUNCTION

System Variables with Axis Names

For system variables with axis names (e.g. VZOF, VRCO), the values for internal axes are read
and written by the A-axis command and those for external axes by the C-axis command. If both A-
and C-axes are on the external pallet, "2213 Alarm B Program bad direct: system variable"
appears.

M-coded Rotary Table Rotating Direction Commands

The M-coded rotary table rotating direction commands are listed below.

4th axis       CW   CCW   Shorter path                        Group No.       Modal
5th axis      M15   M16        M403                                 4
6th axis      M115  M116       M404                                 5
              M331  M332       M405                                56

Even if the C-axis is at the internal position, M-coded rotating direction commands for the A-axis are
observed.

Example:

          If the 4th axis is named A and the 5th named C,

           The M115/M116/M404 commands are internally generated according to the M15/
               M16/M403 commands respectively.
               The M115/M116/M404 commands are effective but are preceded by the internally
               generated M codes.

The default rotary table rotating direction at the time of reset is determined as follows by NC optional
parameter bit No. 69.

bit 0     Shorter path command M403 upon the rest of the 4th            M403  M15
          rotary axis                                                   M404  M115
                                                                        M405  M331
bit 1     Shorter path command M404 upon the rest of the 5th
          rotary axis

bit 2     Shorter path command M405 upon the rest of the 6th
          rotary axis

With this function, the default C-axis rotating direction at the time of reset is the same with the  , and
default A-axis rotating direction.

Example:
                 If the 4th axis is named A and bit 0, NC optional parameter No. 69 is checked

          the 5th axis is named C and bit 1, NC optional parameter No. 69 is not checked                                         ,

          The default rotating directions for both axes will be determined by the shorter path

          command (M403 for the A-axis and M404 for the C-axis).

Mirror Image

The programmable image (G62) should be the same for the A- and C-axis commands.
The mirror image signal issued by a button should also be the same for the A- and C-axes.
                                                                                                                                 6103-E P-58
                                                                     SECTION 4 2-ROTARY TABLE CONTROL FUNCTION

          Plane Selection (G17/G18/G19)

            It is possible to select a rotary axis as an axis on a plane using the plane selection (G17/G18/G19)
            command. If the axis on the selected plane is external, "2256 Alarm B Data word: plane
            specification 6" appears.

4-2-5. Manual Operation

            The axis numbers used for manual operation are fixed regardless of the axis position, internal or
            external.
            If the A- and C-axes correspond with the 4th and 5th axes respectively, for example:
            Use the buttons for the 4th axis to move the A-axis regardless of its position, internal or external.
            Use the buttons for the 5th axis to move the C-axis regardless of its position, internal or external.

          Manual Operation of the Internal Axis

            The external rotary table may be manually operated in the manual operation, manual interruption or
            pulse handle multiplication mode as before.

          Manual Operation of the External Axis

            The external rotary table may be manually operated regardless of the operation mode.
            The shift is not added even in the manual interruption or pulse handle multiplication mode.
            (The axes move in the same manner as during normal manual operation.)

          Manual Movement to the Home Position

            The external axes cannot be manually moved to the home position.

          Functions for Setting the Conditions for Manual Feed of Each Axis

            The external axis can be manually operated regardless of the operation mode.
            The following parameters can be set for each axis
            (1) Rapid feed override
            (2) Pulse handle multiplications
            (3) Manual cutting feedrate (optional/fixed)
            (4) Manual rapid feed selection

4-2-6. Machine Lock

            The machine lock is also effective for the external axis. Moving the external axis with machine lock
            applied changes the current position indicated on the screen but does not actually move the
            machine.
                                                                                                                                 6103-E P-59
                                                                     SECTION 4 2-ROTARY TABLE CONTROL FUNCTION

4-2-7. Tool position offset function

            When the tool position offset function is selected and the control is turned on, the allowable offset
            value set in the tool position offset function control parameter screen in the parameter setting mode
            employs the A-axis allowable offset value for the in-machine axis. The C-axis allowable offset value
            will not be referred to. (This applies when the rotary axis name has not been changed. If changed,
            the changed axis name is used.)
            When the allowable offset value is specified in the machining program, use the address characters
            A and P to the in-machine axis.
            The allowable offset value in the tool position offset function control guide screen in the operation
            mode displays the allowable offset value used for the in-machine axis in the A-axis column.
                                                                         6103-E P-60
             SECTION 4 2-ROTARY TABLE CONTROL FUNCTION

SECTION 5                                                                                                 6103-E P-62
                                                         SECTION 5 INVERSE TIME FEED FUNCTION

           INVERSE TIME FEED FUNCTION

5-1. Overview

            By specifying G93, the inverse time function determines cutting feedrate by specifying the time
            instead of cutting feedrate command.
            There are two methods to specify the time: by specifying cutting time [sec] and by specifying
            FRN=1/F time [min]. These two methods can be switched in the NC optional parameter.

5-2. Specification Details

5-2-1. Inverse Time Feed Function (Specifying the Cutting Time)

Specify the inverse time with the cutting time [sec].
The program including inverse time feed is as follows.

G93           Inverse time feed mode ON
G0 [P01]      Even in the inverse time feed mode, an F command is not necessary in
              positioning control. "P0" represents a coordinate value.
G1 [P02] F?   Specify time [sec] with "F?".
G2 [P03] F?
G3 [P04] F?   Cancel of inverse time feed mode
G94 [or G95]

In the G01 mode, cutting feedrate is determined by "Distance / Time".
In the G02 (or G03) mode, feedrate is determined by "Arc radius / Time".
The cutting feed rate with F0 being issued is the maximum feedrate with the Hi-CutPro / Super-
NURBS control parameter.
A value of F command is the cutting feedrate when an F command is issued with G131.
If any of the following commands is specified in the inverse time feed mode, an alarm occurs: cutting
radius compensation, fixed cycle, area machining, 3D compensation, home position command, and
M code with home position.
If the tool length offset function is used, actual cutting time may differ from the specified cutting time
since the actual tool movement distance differs from the specified distance.

* F commands are not modal in the inverse time feed mode. It is necessary to specify the F
     command each time during the cutting mode.
                                                                                                                                 6103-E P-63
                                                                                SECTION 5 INVERSE TIME FEED FUNCTION

5-2-2. Inverse Time Feed Function (Specifying FRN)

Specify the inverse time with the following formula.
FRN = 1/F time [min]
If the FRN value is out of the range from 0.00001 to 9999.99 [sec], the value is set to either 0.00001
or 9999.99.
If the F command value is out of the range from 0.00001 to 9999.99, an alarm occurs. The time unit
applied with the command follows the setting at DWELL TIME [sec] in the NC optional parameter
(INPUT UNIT SYSTEM).
The program including inverse time feed is as follows.

G93               Inverse time feed mode ON

G1X100S100M3F0.5  The block is executed in 2 minutes. (When "1" is set at
                  DWELL TIME.)

G1X50Y50Z100S100M3F6 The block is executed in 10 seconds. (When "1" is set at
                                         DWELL TIME.)

G3X50Z50I50 K0S100M3F1 In the circular interpolation mode, feedrate is determined
                                         according to the arc radius as explained above.
                                         (FRN = Feedrate / Arc radius)

* F commands are not modal in the inverse time feed mode. It is necessary to specify the F
    command each time during the cutting mode.

5-2-3. Parameter

In the inverse time feed mode, which of the designation above is expressed by an F command is
determined by the setting for NC optional parameter bit No. 54, bit 2.

Check Mark                                                    Description
                  The F command of the inverse time feed function expresses FRN (= 1 / F Time
                  (min)).

                  The F command of the inverse time feed function expresses cutting time (sec).
                                                             6103-E P-64

SECTION 6                                                                                                 6103-E P-66
                                              SECTION 6 SYNCHRONIZED MULTIAXIS FUNCTION

           SYNCHRONIZED MULTIAXIS FUNCTION

6-1. Overview

            This function permits each of multiple pairs of axes to be simultaneously operated by a command for
            a single axis.

6-2. Detailed Specifications

            The synchronized axes mean a pair of axes simultaneously operated by a command for a single
            axis. The one axis is called "master axis" and the other "slave axis."
            The master axis is designated as m and the slave axis as s ( for the name of the synchronized
            axis).
            For the NC capable of controlling five axes, for example, the X-, Y- and Z-axes are simultaneously
            operated as if the machine is controlled with eight axes, Xm, Ym, Zm, B, C, Xs, Ys and Zs, in total.

6-2-1. Synchronized Axis Data Screen

            Press [F8] or [F7] (DISPLAY CHANGE) and select Synchronized Axis Data in the operation screen
            mode to call the following screen.
            A screen page is provided for each synchronized axis and the screen can be paged using the
            [PAGE] key. The total number of available pages is shown at the right top of the screen.

           ME32123R0700800030001
                                                                                                                    6103-E P-67
                                                        SECTION 6 SYNCHRONIZED MULTIAXIS FUNCTION

SYNC OFFSET (synchronized position shift)

Indicates the constant difference in APA between the master and slave axes.
The command value for the slave axis is calculated by deducting the synchronized position shift
from the command value for the master axis.

  (Synchronized position shift) =
                          (APA of master axis in ideal condition) - (APA of slave axis in ideal condition)

  (SRSCONs of slave axis) = (SRSCONm of master axis) - (synchronized position shift)

SYNC ERROR

The master and slave axes in an ideal condition keep a synchronized position shift between them
while moving. If they are put in an undesirable condition (i.e. the one axis is delayed or advanced),
however, a synchronization error occurs.

  (Synchronization error) = (APA of master axis) - (synchronized position shift) - (APA of slave axis)
                                                                                     6103-E P-68
                         SECTION 6 SYNCHRONIZED MULTIAXIS FUNCTION

6-2-2. Axis Data Screen

The axis data table screen shows the data for all the axes including master and slave synchronized
ones.

shift from command values for the master and slave axes at that time.

                                                                        6103-E P-74
            SECTION 6 SYNCHRONIZED MULTIAXIS FUNCTION

6-4. Modes

The synchronized axes are operated in one of the following modes.

(1) Automatic synchronization error correction mode upon power-up

(2) Synchronization error correction mode

(3) Synchronized position shift setting mode

(4) Normal mode

These modes are described below.

(1) Automatic synchronization error correction mode upon power-up
      The slave axis moves in the direction to reduce the synchronization error to "0" upon the power-
      up.
      The moving speed is 93.75 mm/min.
      If the absolute value of the synchronization error before the movement is larger than
      permissible limit B (system parameter), "1246 Alarm A Synchronous error limit over" appears.
      This mode is exited when the absolute value of the synchronization error is reduced to 20 m or
      less.

(2) Synchronization error correction mode
      This mode is intended to erase the excessive synchronization error alarm when it is lit and
      restore the system into the normal condition after the synchronization error is reduced to "0"
      with the pulse handle.
      This mode can be entered by setting bit 0, optional parameter No. 37 to ON.
      Setting and then resetting the parameter bit erases the alarm message "1246 Alarm A
      Synchronous error limit over" and lights the alarm message "2424 Alarm B Synchronous error
      recovering operation."
      The pulse handle set to the multiplication of X1 can be used to only move the synchronized
      master axis in the direction to reduce the synchronization error to "0."
      This mode can be exited by setting bit 0, optional parameter No. 37 to OFF.

(3) Synchronized position shift setting mode
      Before changing the synchronized position shift, set bits 0 and 1, optional parameter No. 37 to
      ON to enter this mode. In this mode, the following additional functions are available in addition
      to those mentioned in (2).

        The axis may also be moved in the direction to increase the synchronization error.

        "Calculating" the (mechanical) origin coordinate of the slave axis as a system parameter in

            this mode also determines the synchronized position shift from command values
            (SRSCONX) for the master and slave axes at that time.

(4) Normal mode
      The normal mode means the status other than described in (1) to (3) where the NC has been
      normally operating since the start-up. In the normal mode, synchronization errors are
      continuously checked for.
      If the absolute value of the synchronization error exceeds permissible limit A (system
      parameter) for approximately 100 ms or longer, the alarm message "1246 Alarm A
      Synchronous error limit over" appears.
                                                                        6103-E P-75

SECTION 7                                                                                                 6103-E P-77
                        SECTION 7 MANUAL FEED FUNCTION IN TOOL AXIAL DIRECTION

           MANUAL FEED FUNCTION IN TOOL AXIAL
           DIRECTION

7-1. Overview

On a slope where the tool axial direction is Z-axis, this function enables rapid traverse, cutting feed,
and pulse handle feed of the X, Y, and Z-axes by manual operation.

This function also displays the actual position in the coordinate system converted from the normal
coordinate system based on the spindle axial direction.

The axis feed method is selectable, by data setting at an NC optional parameter bit, from between
"Manual feed in tool direction" and "Manual feed on slope" when the slope machining manual feed
button is pressed.

7-2. Specification Details

The following parameter bit is used to change the manual feed method.
NC optional parameter bit No. 72, bit 3

No.            bit Status              Description                     Initial value

72             3           Manual feed on slope
                           Manual feed in tool axial direction

Select the manual feed method from between "Manual feed in tool axial direction" and "Manual feed
on slope" when the "any angle feed on/off" switch is turned on.
This parameter is not the power-on effective type.

Even when this parameter is set to ON  (manual feed in tool axial direction), the 3D coordinate

system setting screen can be displayed and used for data setting in the usual manner.

7-2-1. Manual Feed Function in Tool Axial Direction

            On a slope where the tool direction is Z-axis, this function enables rapid traverse, cutting feed, and
            pulse handle feed of the X, Y, and Z-axes by manual operation.

          Manual Feed in Tool Axial Direction

(1) Manual Operation
      After the "any angle feed on/off" switch is turned on, select the required axis (from X, Y, Z-axes
      or those for the pulse handle) with manual feed axis selection key on the machine panel. Then,
      the axis is fed regarding the tool axial direction as the Z-axis by manual feed at rapid traverse,
      manual cutting feed, or pulse handle feed.

(2) Auto Operation / MDI Operation
      During Auto operation or MDI operation, the tool can be moved in its axial direction in the same
      way as the manual operation described in the above item (1). In this case, however, you need
      to select either the manual interruption mode or the pulse handle mode.
                                                                                                                    6103-E P-78
                                   SECTION 7 MANUAL FEED FUNCTION IN TOOL AXIAL DIRECTION
When the "any angle feed on/off" switch is turned on, 3D FEED is displayed on the top of the
screen. At this time, the slope relative actual position is displayed with the coordinates in the
coordinate system converted based on the tool axial direction.
To display the slope relative actual position, feed the page on the ACTUAL POSITION screen.

Points to Be Noted

 The tool axial direction is calculated from the attachment actual position. Therefore, the tool

   direction is recalculated each time the attachment is moved. During this calculation, X, Y, or Z-
   axis feed commands are ignored.

 This function is effective in pulse handle feed done during execution of G69 slope machining

   command.
   Mirror image command does not affect this function.

 When the NC optional parameter bit No. 72 bit 3 is ON           , the relative positions are set in the

coordinate system based on the tool axial direction. The same values are used for position

setting in both the tool direction coordinate system and the slope machining coordinate system.

7-2-2. Manual Feed on Slope

When the NC optional parameter bit No. 72, bit 3 is ON  and the "any angle feed on/off" switch

is ON, the tool is moved according to the manual feed on slope.

For details regarding the manual feed on slope function, refer to "Section 28, Special Functions

Manual No.1".

 NC optional             Mode                Content of Hi-G control
parameter bit

0              5

                         High-acceleration mode Hi-G control executed at the full acceleration

                         Low-vibration mode  Hi-G control executed at the acceleration

                                  SECTION 8 Hi-G FUNCTION IN LOW-VIBRATION MODE

8-3. Parameters

8-3-1. NC Optional Parameter bit No. 67 bit 0

This parameter activates or deactivates the Hi-G function.

No. 67 bit 0                    Makes the Hi-G function active/inactive.

              Active (default)

              Inactive

[Supplement]

    When this parameter is set to "inactive," the parameters described in 8-3-2 and 8-3-3 below

      also become ineffective.

    This parameter is set to "active" at each time of power-up.

8-3-2. NC Optional Parameter bit No. 67 bit 5

This parameter selects the Hi-G function mode from between high-acceleration and low-vibration.

No. 67 bit 5                      Selection of the Hi-G function mode

              High-acceleration mode (default)

              Low-vibration mode

[Supplement]
 This parameter becomes effective at each time of power-up.
                                                                                          6103-E P-87
                          SECTION 8 Hi-G FUNCTION IN LOW-VIBRATION MODE

8-3-3. System Parameters

On the last page of SYSTEM PARAMETERS screen (the page where "PITCH COMP SPAN" and
"NUMBER OF PITCH POINT" parameters are shown), the "G00 ACCEL ADJUST" parameters on
the third line are used to adjust the T2 acceleration for the low-vibration mode of the Hi-G function.
The "G00 ACCEL ADJUST" parameter supports the Hi-G function. It is valid when "1" is set at bit 5
of NC optional parameter bit No. 67 (low-vibration mode).
With this setting, the T2 time constant is used for Hi-G after being divided by 100.

G0 acceleration adjustment factors:

Setting range : 1 to 100 (in %)

Default value : 50 (If the set value is zero, it changes to 50 after the low-vibration mode is
                        selected and the machine is restarted.)

This is a power-on effective parameter.

           FIXED CYCLE TIME REDUCING FUNCTION

9-1. Overview

The following functions have been added to reduce the time required to successively repeat a fixed
cycle.

   Optimum in-position control

   Cycle time reduction for fixed high-speed deep boring (G73) and deep boring (G83) cycles

9-2. Optimum In-position Control

At present, the in-position control (IN-P control) for movement of the feed axes uses in-position
values as system parameters. The in-position check during the fixed cycle sequence is intended to
reduce the in-position waiting time by checking with another in-position value suitable to the
sequence.

9-2-1. Additional Parameters

The following parameters are added to the User Parameters screen:

 (1) CYCLE AXIS INP
 (2) POINT R LEVEL INP
 (3) RETRACT POINT INP
 (4) RETURN POINT INP

Setting range : 0 to 1000 (in units of 0.001 mm)

ADD            : Usable

CAL            : No command

 For each in-position value as a user parameter, see "Fig.9-1".

 If the in-position value as a user parameter is "0," the axes are checked with the in-position

an inclined plane grinding process are checked with the in-position value as a system

parameter.

                                                          SECTION 9 FIXED CYCLE TIME REDUCING FUNCTION

      Ignoring the Step of Spindle Rotation Command (M03)

      The number of cycle steps to be executed can be reduced by skipping the M03 command specified
      step in the fixed cycles, G73/G83/G81/G82/G85/G89/G281.

9-3-1. Making the Spindle Rotation Command (M03) Specified Step
         Ignorable

      An NC optional parameter may be used to make the spindle rotation command (M03) specified step
      ignorable.

               NC optional parameter bit No. 67 bit 1  Effective/ Ineffective

                                                       ME32123R0701100070001

      Note

      The M03 command is not executed with this parameter bit set to "Effective." In this case, the M03
      command should be executed before the G73/G83/G81/G82/G85/G89/G281 block.
      In case of M130 (conditions for spindle rotation off during cutting feed), a cutting operation is
      performed even with the spindle not rotating and the M03 command must be executed before this
      parameter bit is set to "Effective."

9-4. Ignoring the Step of Point R Start Position Command in
        Successive Command Output

      If a fixed cycle is successively repeated in a program as shown below, the point to be reached by the
      second or subsequent repetitions has been reached as far as the previous block is M54 (point R
      level return). The number of cycle steps to be executed can be reduced by jumping the step
      specified by the starting position to point R command.

      N00 G73  X50Y50 R-50 Z-200 P3 F500 M54
               X60Y60
               X70Y70

                    :
                    :

      In the following cases, however, the step specified by the starting position to point R command is not
      ignored.

       The upper limit level return (M52) was commanded in the previous block or the specified point

         level return (M53) has been commanded.

       Point R has been relocated to make the return point in the previous block inconsistent with the

         starting point in the current block.

       During an inclined plane grinding process.

       The local coordinate system has been established (by G10) or canceled (by G11) in the fixed

         cycle mode.

       A changeover was made between absolute (G90) and incremental (G91) in the fixed cycle

         mode.
                                                                                                                    6103-E P-92
                                                    SECTION 9 FIXED CYCLE TIME REDUCING FUNCTION

   The fixed cycle pattern has changed (from G73 to G81, for example) in the fixed cycle mode.

   A fixed cycle with the coordinate calculating function on.

   A G code has been executed in the fixed cycle mode under the condition other than listed

      above.

The conditions for ignoring the command can also be cancelled by G80 or NC reset.

9-4-1. Activating/Deactivating the Function for Ignoring the Step of R-
         point Start Position Command in Successive Command Output

An NC optional parameter can be used to make the step specified by the starting position to point R
command during successive specifications ignorable.

NC optional parameter bit No. 67 bit 2  Effective/ Ineffective

                                                                                                                                                                             ME32123R0701100090001

If the starting position to point R is jumped, the single block is not shut down (by bit 0, NC operation
parameter No. 34) after the movement to the starting position to point R during the single block
operation.

9-5. Activating/Deactivating the Rotation Stop Command
        (M05) at Point Z in the Bottom of G74/G84 Hole

An NC optional parameter can be used to make the rotation stop command (M05) effective/
ineffective at point Z at the bottom of a hole specified by G74/G84.

NC optional parameter bit No. 67 bit 4  Effective/ Ineffective

10-1. Overview

The straight angle compensation function is intended to position an axis using the unit of
measurement by the encoder according the movement of another axis, thus improving the straight
angle.
The error compensation value for the current position of a moving axis is applied to another axis
selected as compensation axis to correct the position of the latter.

10-3. Errors

5456 Straight angle comp. data set over

        The number of straight angle compensation data sets determined when the straight angle
        compensation span is specified exceeds 120.
        [Character-string]
        None
        [Code]
        The calculated number of data sets (hexadecimal)

5458 Straight angle comp. move axis not select

        The moving axis had not been selected when the straight angle compensation span was
        specified.
        [Character-string]
        None

Note

This function does not ensure the machining accuracy for all cases programmable in the
parameter.
The error between back and forth of the movement will be generated according to the parameter
setting value as shown in the following formula.
Emax = Max {|Pn - Pn-1|/L} × F × 0.81

Emax : Max error value between back and forth [0.1m]

F             : Feedrate

L             : Compensation span

n             : Compensation value number

Pn : Compensation value [0.1 m] of compensation value No.nth

Max{} : The function determines the maximum value among the all results of n (1,2,3,...,number
            of data sets)

0.81 : Coefficient
                                                                                                                                 6103-E P-99
                                                                 SECTION 11 ATTACHMENT LOAD DISPLAY FUNCTION

SECTION 11 ATTACHMENT LOAD DISPLAY FUNCTION

11-1. Overview

            The attachment load display function displays the spindle load according to the attachment type.
            The function converts the max load of the currently mounted attachment to 150% and displays the
            spindle load on the actual position display screen and the program display screen.

11-2. Specification Details

11-2-1.Attachment Load Display

The max attachment load is the value set at the attachment parameter as the max load.
Based on this value, the function determines whether it displays the attachment load or the spindle
load. The spindle load means the conventional load display.

Max attachment load             Displayed load
              0                   Spindle load

      Other than 0              Attachment load

11-2-2.Calculating Attachment Load Displayed

            The displayed attachment load is calculated from the value displayed as the spindle load.
            The attachment load calculation formula is as follows:

            Attachment load = Spindle load × 150 / Max load determined by the attachment type

            Example:
            Value displayed as the spindle load: 20 (%)
            Max load of the attachment currently mounted in the spindle: 40

            The attachment load displayed at this time is calculated as shown below:

            Attachment load = 20 × 150 / 40  75 (%)
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-100
                                                                 SECTION 11 ATTACHMENT LOAD DISPLAY FUNCTION

11-2-3.Application to Simple Load Monitor Function

The simple load monitor function monitors the spindle load and generates an alarm when the load
exceeds the value set at the parameter. This function can change the parameter to be set when
using the simple load monitor function between attachment load display and spindle load display by
data setting at the NC optional parameter bit.

   Max load displayed as attachment load

      The displayed max attachment load (hereafter referred to as max AT load) is as follows:

      Max AT load = Current max load × 150 / Max load determined by the attachment type

[Supplement]

 Here, the current max load means the max load rate displayed as the max spindle load.

 Setting the max load rate [%] at the NC optional parameter bit

   The max load rate, which has been set at the spindle overload monitoring parameter, changes
   as shown below depending on the NC optional parameter bit value.

Relevant NC optional parameter bit No.68, bit 4

 Bit setting at NC                               Description
optional parameter

                    The set max load rate [%] becomes the load rate that corresponds to
                    spindle load display.

                    The set max load rate [%] becomes the load rate that corresponds to
                    the attachment load display.

Example:
The max load rate [%] set at the spindle load monitoring parameter currently selected = 100
The max load of attachment currently mounted in the spindle = 40
The max load rate displayed as the spindle load = 100

At this time, the max load displayed as the attachment load [the value displayed at LOAD
MONITOR (%)] is as follows.

 Bit setting at NC  Max load rate
optional parameter

                    375

                    100
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-101
                                                                 SECTION 11 ATTACHMENT LOAD DISPLAY FUNCTION

11-2-4.Application to Synchronized Tapping Torque Monitoring Function

            The synchronized tapping torque monitoring function monitors the load torque applied to the spindle
            during synchronized tapping, and generates an alarm if the set value is exceeded.
            The attachment load display function monitors the load based on the displayed load rate when
            monitoring the synchronized tapping torque.
            Therefore, when the attachment load is displayed, the monitoring torque rate becomes the value
            that corresponds to the attachment load, and it generates an alarm when the displayed attachment
            load exceeds the monitoring torque rate.
            Unlike the simple load monitor function, the attachment load display function does not convert the
            monitoring torque rate by the NC optional parameter bit setting.

                       SECTION 11 ATTACHMENT LOAD DISPLAY FUNCTION

and on the program display screen.

No.  bit                               Description

68   4    In the simple load monitor function that displays the load according
          to the attachment type, the max load rate [%] for the ratio for the
73   5    rated torque command value is set.

          In the simple load monitor function that displays the load according
          to the attachment type, the max load rate [%] for attachment is set.

          The function that displays the load according to the attachment type
          is "valid".

          The function that displays the load according to the attachment type
          is "invalid".
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-104
                                                        SECTION 12 SHORTER PATH COMMAND TO ROTARY TABLE

SECTION 12 SHORTER PATH COMMAND TO ROTARY
                   TABLE

12-1. Overview

The rotary table rotating direction, normal or reverse, is specified by an M-coded command.
With this function, the shorter path rotation can be directed in addition to the clockwise (CW) or
counterclockwise (CCW) rotation.

12-2. Commands

The rotary table rotating direction is specified by an M-coded command, the value of which changes
depending on the rotary table's axis No. (representing the order of indicating the current position).

4th axis rotary table .......... M15 :        CW command
                                      M16 :   CCW command
                                      M403 :  Shorter path rotation command
                                              CW command
5th axis rotary table .......... M115 :       CCW command
                                      M116 :  Shorter path rotation command
                                      M404 :  CW command
                                              CCW command
6th axis rotary table .......... M331 :       Shorter path rotation command
                                      M332 :
                                      M405 :

Note

  When the shorter path command is issued, the rotating direction is determined by the difference

    between the previous and current program command values (angular distance). If the current
    position is not consistent with the program command value as when the coordinate system
    (workpiece or local coordinates) or compensation value (e.g. spindle compensation value) is
    modified or the home position is relocated, the rotary table may rotate by more than 180
    degrees even with the shorter path command.

  If the shorter path command is issued with the angular distance of 180 degrees, the table

    normally rotates.

  If the shorter path command is issued and the home position relocated, the table does not take

    the shorter path but normally rotates.

  The rotary table rotating direction during the sequence return or return through manual

    interruption is determined by the rotation command.

  With the incremental command (G91), the rotating direction command is ineffective. The

    rotating direction conforms to the sign of the command value (+: CW, ­: CCW).

  For the rotary table with limited rotation range, such as a rotary table with limits or a multi-turn

    rotary table, the shorter path rotation command is ineffective. The rotating direction is
    automatically determined so that the table does not exceed the rotation limits.
                                                                                          6103-E P-105
                  SECTION 12 SHORTER PATH COMMAND TO ROTARY TABLE

12-3. Parameters

The type of the rotary table rotation command to be issued upon the NC power-up or reset, normal
or shorter path, can be specified by a parameter.

NC optional parameter bit No. 69

     bit 0 : The initial rotating direction of the 4th axis rotary table is regarded as the direction for
                 shorter path.
                 1 ... Shorter path rotation (M403)
                 0 ... CW (M15)

     bit 1 : The initial rotating direction of the 5th axis rotary table is regarded as the direction for
                 shorter path.
                 1 ... Shorter path rotation (M404)
                 0 ... CW (M115)

     bit 2 : The initial rotating direction of the 6th axis rotary table is regarded as the direction for
                 shorter path
                 1 ... Shorter path rotation (M405)
                 0 ... CW (M331)
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-106
                                                                     SECTION 13 FIXED DEEP HOLE DRILLING CYCLE 2

SECTION 13 FIXED DEEP HOLE DRILLING CYCLE 2

13-1. Overview

            This function uses a fixed cycle equivalent to the conventional deep drilling cycles (G83/G73) except
            that the feedrate and spindle speed gradually decrease during the drilling process.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-107
                                                                     SECTION 13 FIXED DEEP HOLE DRILLING CYCLE 2

13-2. Fixed Deep Hole Drilling Cycle 2 (G281)

            Command format: [G281 X__Y__Z__R__(Q__)I__(J__)F__(P__)
                                                         (QC = __QD = __FA = __FB = __FC = __SC = __KA = __KB = __)]

                                                                              The command values for J, Q, QC, QD, P, KA, KB and
                                                                              SC should not be smaller than zero. Those for I, F, FA,
                                                                              FB and FC should be larger than zero.
                                                                              F F A, FB, FC FB FC S SC

                                                                                                                               Return point

                               Point R
             Initial pecking zone, QC

                          Feed FA
                          Spindle S

                                           J

   Constant speed zone, Q    J

             Feed F

             Spindle S

   Gradual deceleration zone J

                Feed F FB
                Spindle S SC

   Cut-through zone, QD
