              the execution of the schedule  program is       program is

              program.                       terminated.      continued.

Operation while and after the scheduled program is executed follows the above parameter.

Checked (1)   : The execution of the schedule program is terminated. Since the position of the
                schedule pointer does not change, where the interrupt is occurred is known.
                (Even if a count has been specified, the repetition of the main program being
                executed is not continued.)

No check (0) : When the execution of the main program being executed is terminated, the next
                      main program is selected or the counter is decremented if a count has been
                      specified, and machining is continued.

17-4. M Code

Interrupt program validation/invalidation commands (M900, M901, M902, M903, M904, and M905)
The interrupt program becomes the invalidation mode by an M901 to M905 command, and the
specified interrupt program O901 to O905 is executed when the signal is turned off.
The interrupt program becomes the invalidation mode by an M900 command, and nothing occurs
even if the signal is turned off.
In addition, the interrupt program becomes the invalidation mode when the power supply is turned
on, the NC is reset, and the interrupt program starts operation.

M code    Group                          Function              In relation to     Modal/
            53                                                the axis travel   one block
 M900            Interrupt program invalidation command
 M901            Interrupt program validation command (O901)  Simultaneous        Modal
 M902            Interrupt program validation command (O902)  Simultaneous        Modal
 M903            Interrupt program validation command (O903)  Simultaneous        Modal
 M904            Interrupt program validation command (O904)  Simultaneous        Modal
 M905            Interrupt program validation command (O905)  Simultaneous        Modal
                                                              Simultaneous        Modal

17-5. Error

           5433 Interrupt program buffer overflow

                     When the power was supplied, the interrupt program file "A.INT" was not loaded into the
                     memory area dedicatedly used for interrupt programs.
                     [Character-string]
                     None
                     [Code]
                     1->
                     Cause: The interrupt program file "A.INT." is larger than 1024 bytes.
                     Measures: Check the size of the interrupt program file.
                                                                 6103-E P-144
             SECTION 17 INTERRUPT PROGRAM FUNCTION

17-6. Alarm

2561 Alarm B Interrupt program: Bad direct

        The interrupt program is not used in a wrong manner.

        [Index]
        None

        [Character-string]
        None

        [Code]
        1->
        Cause: A command between M900 and M905 is issued during execution of the interrupt
        program.
        Measures: Delete M900 to M905 commands from the interrupt program.
        2->
        Cause: No interrupt program (O901 to O905) is registered.
        Measures: Register the programs O901 to O905 in the interrupt program file (A.INT), then
        resupply the power.
        3->
        Cause: RTI command is used in a program other than the interrupt program.
        Measures: Check and correct the program.

4285 Alarm D Interrupt program

        An interrupt trigger is generated, but the NC failed to normally run the interrupt program.

        [Index]
        None

        [Character-string]
        None

        [Code]
        1->
        Cause: An interrupt trigger is generated during MDI operation mode.
        Measures: Select the automatic operation mode and press the cycle start button to execute
        the interrupt program.
        2->
        Cause: An interrupt trigger is generated during single block, temporary stop, M00 command
        stop, and stop after traveling to the sequence return point.
        Measures: Press the cycle start button to execute the interrupt program.
        3->
        Cause: An interrupt trigger is generated during manual interruption mode.
        Measures: Exit the manual interruption mode and press the cycle start button to execute the
        interrupt program.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-145
                                                                       SECTION 18 SPINDLE PATH CONTROL FUNCTION

SECTION 18 SPINDLE PATH CONTROL FUNCTION

18-1. Overview

            These functions position the spindle or linearly interpolate it in respect with other NC axes. The
            control mode for rotating the spindle with rotation commands is called the spindle speed control
            mode and that by means of linear interpolation, etc. called the spindle path control mode.

18-2. Axis Names

            The spindle subject to move commands in the spindle path control mode (hereinafter, referred to as
            virtual axis) can be named "A," "B" or "C" using the rotary axis parameter in the parameter setting
            mode.
            The spindle is an axis that comes after the NC controlled ones.
            For the system using X-, Y-, Z- and A-axes, for example, the "A-"axis is the fourth axis and the
            spindle is the fifth.
            Before delivery, an alphabet, C, B or A, not used for the NC controlled axes is assigned as the
            spindle name in the shown order of priority.
            Unless otherwise specified, the axis name (virtual axis name) specified by the parameter is used for
            setting the spindle path controlled axis data and program commands and indicating the current
            position.
            The virtual axis position is adjustable in units of 0.001 degree.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                     ME32123R0702100020001
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-146
                                                                       SECTION 18 SPINDLE PATH CONTROL FUNCTION

18-3. Spindle Path Control ON/OFF

The spindle path control mode is entered or existed using a G-coded command.

G154  : Spindle path control OFF

G155 [H] : Spindle path control ON

 With the G155 command, the spindle angle compensation number can be specified after "H."

   The spindle angle compensation is used to change the angle at which the tool is mounted on
   the spindle.
   A value between H0 and H10 can be used. The H value is internally stored by the NC and used
   when the G155 command is issued again. This permits the designer to omit the H value in the
   second or subsequent commands.
   The H value can be reset (to H0) using the reset (including the M02/M30 reset) or by turning the
   power supply off.

Note

The spindle angle compensation is also used for the tool grooving function (optional). When the
tool grooving and spindle path control functions are used at the same time, the H value specified
for tool grooving will also be used for spindle path control until it is reset. The opposite is also true.

   The current position of the virtual axis in the spindle path control mode as selected by the G155

      command depends on the spindle position at the time command was issued. Directly after the
      issue of the G155 command, position the virtual axis with G90 (absolute command) at least
      once.

Other notes

   The axis movement commands issued in the same block with this command are ignored.

   This command (G155/G154) cannot be issued during the tool diameter compensation or a fixed

      cycle. If it is issued, "2610 Alarm B Spindle contour control 1" will appear.
      It cannot be issued either during the tool grooving process (G142). If it is issued, "2610 Alarm B
      Spindle contour control 5" will appear.

   If a spindle movement command is issued in a mode other than G155, "2609- Alarm B Not

      G155 mode" appears (: axis No./name).

   The fixed synchronous tapping cycle (e.g. G284 or G274) cannot be used in the G155 mode. If

      it is issued, "2610 Alarm B Spindle contour control 4" appears.

   If the spindle has not rotated more than a single turn since power-up when the G155 command

      is issued, M19 (spindle index command) is automatically issued by the NC to settle the spindle.
      If this condition occurs with the STM locked, the M19 command automatically generated by the
      NC unit will not be executed and "2610 Alarm B Spindle contour control 7" appears.

   If a command requiring the spindle to be indexed, e.g. M6 for tool change, is issued at the same

      time with G155 (spindle path control mode), the spindle index request from the PLC is ignored,
      resulting in a suspension. When issuing an M6 or similar command, first exit the spindle path
      control mode using the G154 command.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-147
                                                                       SECTION 18 SPINDLE PATH CONTROL FUNCTION

18-4. Spindle Compensation Values

18-4-1.Setting Screen

            Press the [F8] (DISPLAY CHANGE) and select SPINDLE OFFSET in the tool data setting mode to
            call the following screen. The spindle angle compensation numbers to be specified by G155H? and
            the relevant compensation values are shown on the screen. The spindle position represents the
            current spindle angle.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                     ME32123R0702100040001

            These compensation values are also used for tool grooving (optional).
            The spindle position read by the detector is shown. A slight difference may occur between the
            current virtual axis position (specified value).
            If the reverse rotation is directed by the rotary axis parameter, the spindle position will be "360 minus
            the actual position of the virtual axis." A slight difference may also occur in this case.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-148
                                                                       SECTION 18 SPINDLE PATH CONTROL FUNCTION

18-4-2.Setting the Spindle Angle Compensation Values

The values are used to change the angle at which the tool is mounted on the spindle in the spindle
path control mode. There are eleven compensation values. Note that the compensation value for
NO. 0 shown on the spindle compensation screen in the tool data setting mode will be added to the
values for NOs. 1 to 10. See the following table.

Command   Compensation value(s) used

A G155H0  Value for spindle angle compensation NO. 0

B G155H1 to H10 Value for spindle angle compensation NO. 0 + value for the spindle angle
                             compensation number corresponding to the H value

C G155    Compensation value for A: Used when the G155H? command has not
                                               been issued since power-up and NC reset.

          Compensation value for B: Used when G155H? command has been
                                               issued since power-up and NC reset.
                                               (Compensation value for A is used in case of
                                               H0.)

The data setting procedure is described below.

Setting a Spindle Compensation Value in the Tool Data Setting Mode

Procedure :

  1 Call the spindle compensation screen in the tool data setting mode. Move the cursor to a

      desired value field.

  2 Set the value.

        (a) If the compensation value is known:
             Press [F1] (SET) and enter the compensation value from the keyboard.

        (b) If a prior compensation value has been entered and the difference from the value is known:
             Press [F2] (ADD) and enter the difference from the keyboard.

        (c) Tool angle offset
             Position the spindle at a desired angle with the tool mounted on it. Press [F3] (CAL). After
             power-down and power-up, always rotate the spindle more than a single turn and press
             [F3] (CAL). The compensation value is not defined until then.

      If the coordinate of the virtual axis's mechanical origin or workpiece origin is not zero, enter the
      sum of their coordinates using numeral keys. (See Note 1.)

  3 Press the WRITE key.

      The compensation value is indicated at the cursor position on the screen.

  4 When using this compensation value for the program, set G155 to "H?." (? Represents the

      relevant spindle angle compensation NO.)
      The value set for spindle compensation NO. 0 corresponds with the spindle compensation
      value indicated as optional parameter long word NO. 29.
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-149
                                                          SECTION 18 SPINDLE PATH CONTROL FUNCTION

      [Supplement]

        The currently selected spindle angle compensation numbers are headed by "." The
        spindle angle compensation NO. 0 is always added to the value for any selected spindle
        compensation number and therefore it is always headed by "."
        Data setting range: 0 to 359.999 (min data setting unit: 1 degree)

        Note 1: If the reverse rotation is directed by the rotary axis parameter, calculate the value by
        the following formula:
        "360 ­ (Spindle position) ­ (Machine zero) ­ (workpiece origin)" (in degrees)
        Then, press [F1] (SET).

Setting with Optional Parameter Long Word No. 29:

The set value corresponds with the value for spindle compensation NO. 0 shown on the spindle
compensation screen in the tool data setting mode.

Procedure :

  1 Call the optional parameter long word in the parameter setting mode and move the cursor to

      No.29 field.

  2 Set the value.

        (a) If the compensation value is known:
             Press [F1] (SET) and enter the compensation value from the keyboard.

        (b) If a prior compensation value has been entered and the difference from the value is known:
             Press [F2] (ADD) and enter the difference from the keyboard.

        (c) Tool angle offset
             Position the spindle at a desired angle with the tool mounted on it. Press [F3] (CAL). After
             power-down and power-up, always rotate the spindle more than a single turn and press
             [F3] (CAL). The compensation value is not defined until then.

  3 Press the WRITE key. The value is programmable in units of 1/1000 degree.

      When using this compensation value for the program, set G155 to "H0."

[Supplement]

 Data setting range: 0 to 359999
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-150
                                                                       SECTION 18 SPINDLE PATH CONTROL FUNCTION

18-5. Spindle Movements during Spindle Path Control

            Essentially the same as with the NC round table.

18-5-1.Program Commands

               Use the address character for the virtual axis. The value is programmable in units of 0.001

                   degree.

               The value is programmable between ­360.000 and +360.000.

               The rotating direction is the same with ordinary rotary axes and determined by the M-coded

                   command.

               During any commanded movement, compensation values are added to the coordinates of the

                   mechanical origin (system parameter), workpiece origin, etc, as with ordinary rotary axes. The
                   spindle compensation value (as mentioned above) is also added.
                   The spindle compensation value is added to the tool length compensation value. It is only
                   added for the virtual axis.
                   Target position (detector coordinates) = Mechanical origin + workpiece origin + attachment
                   compensation value + tool compensation value + spindle compensation value

18-5-2.Indications

               The current position of the virtual axis is indicated as "-----" when not in the spindle path control

                   mode. The shift is included in the current position.

               In the spindle path control mode, correct values of RDIF, ODIF, RCON, RAPA, RSAPA and load

                   factor of the virtual axis are indicated. Other parameter values are variable (normally, zero).
                   When not in the spindle control mode, the values listed above (e.g. RDIF) are also variable
                   (normally, zero).

               The load on the virtual axis is fed in the same manner for the MOP-TOOL.

18-5-3.Data Setting

               The workpiece origin and user and system parameters can be set and used for operation,

                   whether in the spindle path control mode or not. The calculating function operates even if the
                   spindle path control mode is not selected. However, such calculation is meaningless because of
                   the variable current position.

               The virtual axis backlash as specified by the user parameter is forcedly changed to 0.000. The

                   value contained in the spindle servo data is used as virtual axis backlash.

               With the spindle path control function, the "spindle compensation data" can be viewed and

                   modified on the tool data setting screen. The data is commonly used for the tool grooving
                   function.
                                                                                          6103-E P-151
                                  SECTION 18 SPINDLE PATH CONTROL FUNCTION

18-5-4.Other Functions

Multi-turn Rotary Table Function

   The multi-turn round table type is available. However, it cannot be used as cylindrical side

      grinding axis.

   If the spindle path control mode is selected with G155, define the current position to have the

      workpiece coordinates between 0.000 and 359.999.

Animated Graphics (Optional)

   The virtual axis cannot be selected as rotary axis (not covered by the drawing data).
   The virtual axis cannot be used to define the graphic coordinates.

Machine Lock

   The movements are permitted even with the machine locked.

Sequence Restart

   The sequence return can be normally performed.

   If the spindle does not pass by the spindle marker in the sequence return block, an alarm is

      raised. Before starting, rotate the spindle by more than a single turn.

Manual Interruption

The manual interruption is also permitted in the spindle path control mode (with G155 issued).

(1) Entering the manual interruption mode
      The spindle is put in the speed control mode. With the spindle pulse handle effective, it is put in
      the pulse handle feed mode.

(2) Recovery through the manual interruption

        Recovery through the manual interruption puts the NC back in the spindle path control

            mode and returns the virtual axis to the position where it was before the entry into the
            manual interruption mode, regardless of the spindle status during the manual interruption.

(3) Exiting the manual interruption mode

        Exiting the manual interruption mode puts the NC back in the spindle path control mode

            and adds the shift calculated from the virtual axis position before the entry into the manual
            interruption mode and the current virtual axis position, to the cumulative shift, regardless of
            the spindle status during the manual interruption.

Spindle Operating Hours (Optional)

If the spindle rotates even during the spindle path control, it is counted as operating.
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-152
                                                          SECTION 18 SPINDLE PATH CONTROL FUNCTION

Spindle Overload Monitor Function (Optional)

The spindle is not checked for overload during the spindle path control.

18-6. Parameters

When a checkmark is set at the NC optional parameter (bit) No. 73 bit 6, the virtual axis (NC rotary
table axis that corresponds to the spindle) is movable under the spindle path control, regardless of
the n-th axis positive interlock and n-th axis negative interlock signals for the NC-PLC interface.

NC Optional Parameter Bit

No.           bit                                       Description
                           Enables the virtual axis interlock under the spindle path control.
73            6            Disables the virtual axis interlock under the spindle path control.

 The NC optional parameter bit No. 73 bit 6 is not a power-ON effective parameter.

                                                                        SECTION 19 100/20 SETS OF G/M CODE MACROS

SECTION 19 100/20 SETS OF G/M CODE MACROS

19-1. Overview

            These functions are the same with the standard G/M code macro functions except that the number
            of G code macros has been increased from 20 to 100 sets and that of M code macros from 10 to 20
            sets.

            The numbers of macro sets can be increased in one of three ways depending on the NC optional
            parameter settings.

                  1. 100 sets of G code macros (10 for MODIN and 90 for CALL) and 20 sets of M code macros
                  2. 100 sets of G code macros (90 for MODIN and 10 for CALL) and 20 sets of M code macros
                  3. 100 sets of G code macros (50 for MODIN and 50 for CALL) and 20 sets of M code macros

            The functions are the same as with the standard G/M code macros.
            See "G and M code Macro Functions" in Section 10 of Programming Manual.

19-2. Details

19-2-1.Numbers of Additional G/M code Macros

            In addition to the standard G/M code macros:
              G code macros, MODIN type: G101 to G110
              G code macros, CALL type: G111 to G120
              M code macros, CALL type: M201 to M210

            G /M code macros can be added in one of three ways:

            (1) 80 sets of G code macros (CALL) and 10 sets of M code macros added
                   Standard macros plus
                   G code macros, CALL type: G501 to G580
                   M code macros, CALL type: M211 to M220

            (2) 80 sets of G code macros (MODIN) and 10 sets of M code macros added
                   Standard macros plus
                   G code macros, MODIN type: G501 to G580
                   M code macros, CALL type: M211 to M220

            (3) 40 sets of G code macros (CALL), 40 sets of G code macros (MODIN) and 10 sets of M code
                   macros added
                   Standard macros plus
                   G code macros, MODIN type: G501 to G540
                   G code macros, CALL type: G541 to G580
                   M code macros, CALL type: M211 to M220

            The numbers of additional macros depend on the settings of the NC optional parameter bits
            mentioned below.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-156
                                                                        SECTION 19 100/20 SETS OF G/M CODE MACROS

19-2-2.Changing the Numbers of Additional Macros

The numbers of G /M code macros are determined by the settings of bits 3 and 4 for NC optional
parameter No. 73.

            No.73         Content

     Bit 4         Bit 3

(1)                       10 sets of MODIN type G code macros, 90 sets of CALL type ones
                          and 20 sets of M code macros

(2)                       90 sets of MODIN type G code macros, 10 sets of CALL type ones
                          and 20 sets of M code macros

(3)                       50 sets of MODIN type G code macros, 50 sets of CALL type ones
                          and 20 sets of M code macros

 Settings of bits 3 and 4 for NC optional parameter No. 73 are not changed by repeated power-

   ups.

 Changing the settings of bits 3 and 4 for NC optional parameter No. 73 changes the manner of

   calling the additional G code macros (G501 to G580), possibly resulting in a malfunction of the
   machining program.

            "Numbers of Additional G/M Code Macros."

                                                                         SECTION 20 TOOL LIFE PREDICTION FUNCTION

SECTION 20 TOOL LIFE PREDICTION FUNCTION

20-1. Overview

            This function activates the status indicators giving a tool life prediction. At the same time, it raises
            alarm D to warn the operator that a tool group is reaching the end of its life.
             The tool life indicators include the status indicator lamp and warning buzzer.

20-2. Function

20-2-1.Tool Life Prediction

            When the total remaining number of tools in the same tool group No. of the active tool is reduced to
            the prediction number (warning point), the status indicators are activated and alarm D is raised to
            give a tool life prediction.
            "4286 Alarm D: Tool life previous notice"

            In the TOOL DATA mode, specify the prediction number (warning point) for each tool group No.
            between 1 and 999 on the "WARNING POINT SET" screen as shown below.

                                                                                                                                                                                           ME32123R0702300020001

                       Fig.20-1 TOOL DATA SETTING: WARNING POINT SET screen

Tools with the tool group No. set to 0 are not covered by the tool life prediction function.
Therefore, assign a group No. (between 1 and 999) to each tool.

Parameter  Title                    Description             Effective  Programmable
     No.                                                   command            range
                    Specify the prediction number at
1 to 999   WARNING  which the status indicators are to be  SET         0 to 32767
                    activated.
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-160
                                                            SECTION 20 TOOL LIFE PREDICTION FUNCTION

[Supplement]

 1) The remaining number of tools in the same tool group No. of the active tool represents the
      remaining value displayed when the management mode has been set at the specified number.

 2) For the tools needing the tool life prediction, set the management mode at the specified
      number. The management mode set at no life control or warning by time is not covered by the
      function.

 3) For the tool group No. set at the specified number and not at the specified number, only the
      tools specified are covered.

 4) For NG tools, the remaining number is considered as zero.
 5) The tools not listed in the "Pot/Tool Correspondence Table" on the MAGAZINE INFO. sheet

      are not covered by the function.

 6) If the active tool has not been specified or has the group No. of zero, the tool life prediction will
      not be given.

 7) When the tool life is managed in terms of number, the TLFON and TLCO commands are
      necessary.
      Example:
             TLFON
             TLCO Q XXX
      XXX represents a number from 0 to 999 to be counted down.
      If this number is not specified, only one will be counted down. For detail, see SECTION 21
      "Tool Life Management Functions" of Special Functions Manual No. 1.

 8) It is possible to choose to or not to display the alarm "4286 Alarm D: Tool Life Previous Notice"
      using NC optional parameter No. 76 bit 6.
      If it is ON, the tool life prediction alarm will not be displayed. If it is OFF, the tool life prediction
      alarm will be displayed (default).

 9) With the parameter output function, the warning point data can be read and defined with P No.
      "240."

             SECTION 20 TOOL LIFE PREDICTION FUNCTION

                                                                              SECTION 21 TOOL LIFE WARNING FUNCTION

SECTION 21 TOOL LIFE WARNING FUNCTION

21-1. Overview

            This function activates the status indicators to show the tool group has reached the end of life. At the
            same time, it raises an alarm C to warn the operator of the tool life
             The tool life indicators include the status indicator lamp and warning buzzer.

21-2. Function

21-2-1.Tool Life Warning

            Among active tools, if all of the tools in the same tool group number are turned to NG, the status
            indicator lamp for tool life prediction goes off. Then the status indicator lamp for tool life warning
            comes on to display the alarm C.
            "3277 Alarm C Tool life management no spare tool"

            When above alarm C is raised, change the tool to new one. Set the specified number and remained
            number of the tool on the screen and set OK in OK/NG column.
            If the tool change command is executed for the tool group number where has no spare tool without
            changing the tool to new one, the following alarm B will occur.
            "2436 Alarm B Active tool NG2"

            [Supplement]

              1) All tools in the same tool group No. of the active tool represents tools in all modes.
              2) The tools not listed in the "Pot/Tool Correspondence Table" on the MAGAZINE INFO. sheet

                   are not covered by the function.
              3) Tool life warning will not be activated when the active tool is not set.
              4) If the group number of active tool is set to 0, the tool life warning is given only for the active

                   tool.
              5) You can set whether displaying the alarm "3277 Alarm C Tool life management no spare tool"

                   or not in the NC optional parameter bit No.76 bit 7.
                   If it is ON, the tool life prediction alarm will not be displayed. If it is OFF, the tool life prediction
                   alarm will be displayed (default).

             SECTION 21 TOOL LIFE WARNING FUNCTION

            [Index]

            Example)

                                                                                                                                                                                             ME32123R0702600010001

            This specification becomes effective with the following NC optional parameter bit ticked .

                                                                                         SECTION 23 USER TASK 3 FUNCTION

SECTION 23 USER TASK 3 FUNCTION

23-1. Unconditional Branch (GOTO Statement)

[Function]
Branch function always taken to jump
This function cannot be used in the main program selected with S operation method.

[Command format]

GOTO_  <Expression>

       In <expression>, you can set the variables such as common variables, system variables
       and local variables.
       If the value of <expression> is a real number, the value is returned as the rounded
       integer.
       If the commanded sequence number is not found, the alarm 2223 occurs. As the
       sequence number has to be within 5 digits, if the value is other than 1 to 99999, the alarm
       2223 occurs.
       Always place a space between GOTO and <expression>.
       Example 1) When the value of VC1 is 230.4, "GOTO VC1" jumps to the sequence
       number N230.
       Example 2) When the value of VNTOL is 5, "GOTO VNTOL" jumps to the sequence
       number N5.
       Example 3) When the value of VC1 is 3 and the value of VC2 is 7, "GOTO VC1VC2"
       jumps to the sequence number N21.

Note

The value for the sequence number where the GOTO statement jumped to is converted to a
number.
Therefore, be careful since the "GOTO <expression>" cannot jump to the sequence number which
has 0 next to N, such as N0005.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-167
                                                                                         SECTION 23 USER TASK 3 FUNCTION

23-2. Conditional Branch (IF Statement)

            [Function]
            This function executes the conditional task. If the condition is met, the task commanded after THEN
            is executed. If not met, it goes to next sequence.
            This function cannot be used in the main program selected with S operation method.

            [Command format]

             IF [<conditional expression>] THEN_<execute>
                         There are six <conditional expression>s; LT, LE, EQ, NE, GT and GE.
                         The difference from the standard IF statement is that the macros can be commanded with
                         description THEN after <conditional expression>. Others are same as the standard IF
                         statement.
                         For details, see "11-1-1. Branch Function" in "Section 11 User Task Function" in the
                         "Programming Manual."
                         Always place a space between THEN and <execute>.
                         Example 1) When the value of VC1 is 10 and the value of VC5 is 0, the value of VC5 is
                         given as 10 by executing [IF [VC1 GT 3]THEN VC5=VC1].
                         Example 2) When the value of VC2 is 10 and the value of VC10 is 0, the value of VC10
                         remains as 0 after executing [IF[VC2 GT 15]THEN VC10=VC2].

23-3. Iteration (WHILE Statement)

            [Function]
            Write a WHILE statement followed by a conditional expression.
            The program carries out the task between DO and END while the condition is met.
            If the condition is not met, it goes to the sequence next to END.
            DO - END loop cannot be used in the main program selected with B or S operation method.

            [Command format]

             WHILE [<conditional expression>] DO_m
             <execute>
             END_m

                         There are six <conditional expression>s; LT, LE, EQ, NE, GT and GE.
                         They are same as the conditional expression of the standard IF statement.
                         For details, see "11-1-1. Branch Function" in "Section 11 User Task Function" in the
                         "Programming Manual."
                         Specify 1, 2, 3 as m (identification number). An alarm will occur if a number other than
                         those is specified.
                         Always place a space between DO and END or m.

                         Example) The value of VC1 is returned as 45 and VC2 as 10 with the following program.
                         O0001
                         VC1=0 VC2=0
                         WHILE[VC2 LT 10]DO 1
                         VC1=VC1+VC2
                         VC2=VC2+1
                         END 1
                         M02
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-168
                                                                            SECTION 23 USER TASK 3 FUNCTION

[Infinite loop]
If only DO_m is commanded, omitting WHILE <conditional expression>, an infinite loop may occur
between DO and END. So be careful in handling.

            Example) Both VC1 and VC2 are kept added with the following program.
            O0001
            VC1=0 VC2=0
            DO 1
            VC1=VC1+VC2
            VC2=VC2+1
            END 1
            M02

[Multiplexing WHILE]
Basically, the identification number (1 to 3) for DO - END can be used as many times as needed. It
is possible to multiplex WHILE up to three nests. If a DO - END loop command is overlapped with
each other, an alarm may occur. An alarm will occur if two or more DOs with the same identification
number exist in a DO- END loop.

            Example 1) The value of VC3 is returned as 5, VC4 as 9 and VC5 as 10 with the following
            program.
            O0001
            VC3=0 VC4=20 VC5=0
            WHILE[VC5 LT 10]DO 2
            WHILE[VC3 LT 5]DO 1
            WHILE[VC4 GE 10]DO 3
            VC4=VC4-1
            END 3
            VC3=VC3+1
            END 1
            VC5=VC5+1
            END 2
            M02

            Example 2) The following case causes an alarm because "DO 2 - END 2" and "DO 1 - END
            1" overlaps each other.
            WHILE[VC5 GT 10]DO 2
            WHILE[VC3 GT 5]DO 1
            VC3=VC3+1
            END 2
            VC5=VC5+1
            END 1

            Example 3) The following case causes an alarm because there is an extra "DO 2" in "DO 2
            - END 2".
            WHILE[VC5 GT 10]DO 2
            WHILE[VC3 GT 5]DO 2
            VC3=VC3+1
            END 2
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-169
                                                                            SECTION 23 USER TASK 3 FUNCTION

[DO - END loop and jump]
If the command is made to jump into DO - END loop from outside of the loop, an alarm will occur.
Conversely, the command to jump out of the DO - END loop is acceptable. In that case, the DO -
END loop of same identification number cannot be used in the subsequent program.

            Example 1) The value of VC1 is returned as 45 and VC2 as 10 with the following program.
            O0001
            VC1=0 VC2=0
            DO 1
            VC1=VC1+VC2
            VC2=VC2+1
            IF[VC2 GE 10]N123
            END 1
            N123 M02

            Example 2) The following case causes an alarm because program commands to jump into
            DO 1 - END 1 loop.
            GOTO N123
            DO 1
            VC3=VC3+1
            N123 VC5=VC4
            END 1
                                                   6103-E P-170
             SECTION 23 USER TASK 3 FUNCTION

23-4. Alarm

2637 Alarm B No WHILE, IF-THEN, GOTO function

WHILE statement, GOTO <expression> or IF/THEN statement is designated although WHILE
statement function is not provided.

[Character-string]
None

[Code]
1: WHILE is designated.
2: GOTO <expression> is designated.
3: IF/THEN is designated.

[Probable Faulty Locations]
Any of the commands indicated with the above codes is specified in NC program.

[Measures to Take]
Change the NC program by eliminating the command indicated with the codes.

2626 Alarm B WHILE command error

"WHILE", "DO" or "END" command is wrongly specified or the condition judgment data is erroneous
in "Iteration (WHILE statement)."

[Character-string]

None

[Code]

1: No "[" after "WHILE"
2: The value inside "[", "]" stated with "WHILE" is not theoretical number.
3: "DO_m" is not found after "WHILE [statement]".
4: Range is over "m" in "DO_m" or "END_m".
5: "END_m" which corresponds to "DO_m" is not found. Or the "WHILE" statement is used in the B
or S operation method.
6: "DO_m" which corresponds to "END_m" is not found.
7: The loop "DO_m-END_m" overlaps.
8: "DO_m-END_m" loop is to be jumped in.
9: The same identifier number "DO_m" is found in "DO_m-END_m" loop.

[Probable Faulty Locations]

1: Statement error, no "[" after "WHILE" statement.
2: Statement error, the value inside "[", "]" stated with "WHILE" is not theoretical number.
3: Statement error, "DO_m" is not found after "WHILE [statement]".
4: Only 1, 2 or 3 can be attempted to "m" in "DO_m-END_m".
5: "END_m" (DO_m) corresponds to "DO_m" (END_m) is not found. Or the main program is
selected with B or S operation method.
6: "END_m" (DO_m) corresponds to "DO_m" (END_m) is not found.
7: The loop "DO_m-END_m" overlaps with another "DO_m-END_m".
8: Branching command ("GOTO" or the like) is made to cut in "DO_m-END_m" loop.
9: "END_m" which corresponds to "DO_m" is not found.

SECTION 24 MANUAL ANGLE FEED/CIRCLE FEED
                   FUNCTION

24-1. Overview

            These functions allow the operator to perform angle and circle feeds by controlling two-axis
            simultaneously according to the data set up on the exclusive Data Setting screens. To perform that,
            the operator uses the pulse handles or manual feed switches in the manual operation mode
            (including when operation has been manually interrupted or the playback function executed).
            The behaviors of and procedures for "angle feed" and "circle feed" are described below.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-172
                                                      SECTION 24 MANUAL ANGLE FEED/CIRCLE FEED FUNCTION

24-2. Manual Angle Feed Function

24-2-1.Outline of the Operation

            This function allows the operator to simultaneously control two axes according to the plane and
            angle settings on the exclusive Data Setting screen, using the pulse handles or manual feed
            switches.
            For example, move the Y-axis using the pulse handle or manual feed switch with the following
            settings on the Data Setting screen. Then the X-axis moves by the amount automatically determined
            from the Y-axis moving amount to make an angle feed at 30°.

                                 ME32123R0702800020001

Y-axis motion made by operating  30 degrees
Y-axis pulse handle or manual
feed switch

                                 X-axis motion automatically controlled when
                                 Y-axis pulse handle or manual feed switch is
                                 operated

                                                                                                                  ME32123R0702800020002
                                                                                           6103-E P-173
                  SECTION 24 MANUAL ANGLE FEED/CIRCLE FEED FUNCTION

24-2-2.Procedure

Setting Procedure on the Data Setting Screen

You can make the function effective/ineffective and preset parameters on the exclusive Data Setting
screen. This subsection describes the procedure for setting up the data on the Data Setting screen.

Procedure :

  1 Switch to the manual operating state.

      This function is only effective when manual operations are permitted. Switch to the manual
      operating state in advance. Manual operations are permitted under any of the following
      conditions.

      Manual operating state:

         The manual operation mode has been selected.

         The automatic or MDI operation has been manually interrupted.

         The playback function has been executed (in the programmed operation mode).

      [Supplement]

        If the manual operations are not permitted, the data can be set up on the Data Setting
        screen but the function remains ineffective. Switch to the manual operating state in advance.

  2 Confirm the function menu.

      Check that (ANGLE/CIRCLE) is shown at the [F7] position in the function menu.

                                              ME32123R0702800030001
                                                                                                                6103-E P-174
                                      SECTION 24 MANUAL ANGLE FEED/CIRCLE FEED FUNCTION

    Press the function key [F7] (ANGLE/CIRCLE) to switch to the Data Setting screen for this
    function, which contains the following information.

                                             (7)

                           (2)

24-2-3.Notes

(1) This function can only be effectuated in the manual operation mode (including the manual
      interruption mode and when the playback function has been executed). In other modes, the
      Data Setting screen can be viewed but the function cannot be effectuated by selecting "ANGL"
      for "FEED SELECT." If you do so, error message 9434 "Now is not MANUAL MODE (Include
      MID AUTO MANUAL)" appears. If it appears, check the operation mode.

(2) The axes other than selected for "PLANE SELECT" on the Data Setting screen can be
      simultaneously operated with a operated or orthogonal axis as well as independently operated.

(3) If YZ plane is selected for "PLANE SELECT," the relationship between the operated axis and
      the feeding direction may become opposite depending on the machine type. For detail, see the
      documents issued by the machine manufacturer.

(4) The function automatically becomes "ineffective" under any of the following conditions.

        Upon the power-on

        The manual operation mode is changed to another mode (automatic or MDI).

        The manual interruption initiated in the automatic or MDI operation mode is discontinued.

        The playback function as an interactive program function is quitted.

(5) The mirror image function is not related with this function and, therefore, gives no effect.

(6) If the one axis reaches the software limit during manual angle feed, the other axis stops at the
      same time to prevent the former axis from moving out of the limit. In this case, the status
      indicator lamp "LIMIT" lights.

(7) If the two axes selected for "PLANE SELECT" are simultaneously operated, only the one axis is
      operated as shown below.
      XY plane.........Only Y-axis is operated.
      XZ plane.........Only Z-axis is operated.
      YZ plane.........Only Y-axis is operated.

(8) The manual rapid feed is prevented during the manual angle feed.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-180
                                                      SECTION 24 MANUAL ANGLE FEED/CIRCLE FEED FUNCTION

24-3. Manual Circle Feed Function

24-3-1.Outline of the Operation

            This function allows the operator to simultaneously control two axes using the pulse handle or
            manual feed switch for Y-axis to perform a circle feed on the XY plane (this is impossible on other
            planes). The function uses the following data; the center position data defined on the exclusive Data
            Setting screen and the radius determined from that data and the actual position.
            For example, move the Y-axis using the pulse handle or manual feed switch with following settings
            on the Data Setting screen. Then the X-axis moves by the amount automatically calculated from the
            Y-axis moving amount to make a circular motion.
            The direction of the actual circular motion (clockwise or counterclockwise) depends on the Y-axis
            moving direction (positive/negative).

[When Y-axis is moved in positive direction, for example]             ME32123R0702800070001

                                                           Actual position
                                                            Radius

24-3-3.Notes

(1) This function can only be effectuated in the manual operation mode (including the manual
      interruption mode and when the playback function has been executed). In other modes, the
      Data Setting screen can be viewed but the function cannot be effectuated by selecting "CRCL"
      for "FEED SELECT." If you do so, error message 9434 "Now is not MANUAL MODE (Include
      MID AUTO MANUAL)" appears. If it appears, check the operation mode.

(2) The function automatically becomes "ineffective" under any of the following conditions.

        Upon the power-on

        The manual operation mode is changed to another mode (automatic or MDI).

        The manual interruption initiated in the automatic or MDI operation mode is discontinued.

        The playback function as an interactive program function is quitted.

(3) If the one axis reaches the software limit during manual circle feed, the other axis stops at the
      same time to prevent the former axis from moving out of the limits. In this case, the status
      indicator lamp "LIMIT" lights.

(4) It is also possible to simultaneously operate multiple axes using the pulse handles or manual
      feed switches.

(5) If the speed specified by the pulse handle or manual feed switch is too high and therefore
      corrected, alarm 4204 "Feedrate command limit over (replacing)" is raised.
      The corrected speed depends on the radius.

(6) The mirror image function is not related with this function and, therefore, gives no effect.

(7) The manual rapid feed is prevented during the manual circle feed.

(8) The actual radius shown on the Data Setting screen is calculated as distance from the actual
      position to the center. The actual position may be instable in the order of microns, which
      possibly makes the actual radius also instable and inconsistent with the preset radius.

(9) Starting point A and returning point A' may have different coordinates even if the manual circle
      feed is programmed to return to the original position along the periphery (A  B  A').

              Starting point

              Returning point
              Target point

In this case, however, the center position specified on the Data Setting screen does not change.

SECTION 25 B-AXIS I/L WITH 2 ROTARY TABLES

25-1. Overview

            For the machine with 2-pallet APC, an additional axis controlled by communications is mounted on
            the pallet. This function limits the B-axis travel for rotating the pallet to protect the B-axis wiring and
            the piping on the pallet.

25-2. Setting Parameters

            The B-axis interlock screen shown on the next page is added, allowing you to set the B-axis rotation
            range on the screen displayed in the parameter setting mode.
            (The rotation range is regarded as the same with the positive and negative limits set at the system
            parameters.)
            The B-axis rotation range is used only when either the 5th or the 6th axis or both are connected.

            Set the required data in the procedure below:

            Procedure :

           1 Select the parameter setting mode.

                   If the following screen (Fig.1) does not appear, press [F8] (DISPLAY CHANGE) and select B
                   AXIS INTERLOCK.

           2 The screen shows the parameters B (A/L pallet) and B (B/R pallet). The former is used to set

                   the B-axis rotation range applied when the L pallet is in the machine and the latter is used to
                   set the B-axis rotation range applied when the R pallet is in the machine.
                   Each parameter includes the positive limit value (P-limit) and the negative limit value (N-limit)
                   represented in the machine coordinate system.

           3 Setting

                     When the limit value is known

                         Press the function key [F1] (SET) and enter the P and N limit data through the keyboard.

                     If the P and N limit data has been already set and the difference from the current value is

                         known
                         Press the function key [F2] (ADD) and enter the difference through the keyboard.

                     If newly entering a relative position from the actual position (currently located B-axis

                         position in the machine coordinate system)
                         Press the function key [F3] (CAL) and enter the relative amount through the keyboard.
                         Press the WRITE key.
                         The cursor-located position data is rewritten.

                   Setting range: -9999.9999 to 9999.9999
                                                                                               6103-E P-192
                                         SECTION 25 B-AXIS I/L WITH 2 ROTARY TABLES

                                                     ME32123R0702900020001

25-3. B-axis Interlock Function

The B-axis is switched between unlimited rotary axis and multi-turn axis as shown below depending
on the connection/disconnection state of the 5th axis and the 6th axis and the value set at the NC
optional parameter (bit) No.39, bit 4.

Connection/disconnection of 5th and               NC optional parameter bit No. 39, bit 4
                    6th axes
                                         1/0  Multi-turn axis
    Either one or both are connected.    1  Multi-turn axis
                                         0  Unlimited rotary axis
       Neither of them is connected.

Set adequate values at the following parameters:

 P-limit and N-limit on the B-axis interlock screen

 NC optional parameter bit No.39, bit 4
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-193
                                                                         SECTION 25 B-AXIS I/L WITH 2 ROTARY TABLES

25-3-1.B-axis Rotation Limit (When 5th or 6th, or Both Axes Are
         Connected)

            (1) When the A-pallet is in the machine
                   The B-axis can rotate only in the range set at the system parameter B (A/L pallet).
                    Note: If, however, the B-axis is an index table, the B-axis command value is rounded to an
                              index angle. If, for example, the table is indexed in units of 1°, the fractions below the
                              decimal point are rounded off. Therefore, an alarm occurs if the limit is 400.0000 and
                              the command value is 399.5000.

                   Rotation range: N-limit of A/L pallet  B-axis machine coordinate  P-limit of A/L pallet

                    In the automatic or MDI operation mode, the machine generates the following alarm on

                         receiving a B-axis command for movement to the outside of the rotation range.
                         When B-axis is located outside the rotation range, B-axis command, whether to the rotation
                         range or to the outside of rotation range, causes the following alarm.
                         (This alarm occurs only when an improper B-axis command is issued.)

                         If the command value exceeds the P-limit
                         2268 Alarm-B Plus travel limit over B-AXIS 8

                         If the command value exceeds the N-limit
                         2269 Alarm B Minus travel limit over B-AXIS 8

                    When the B-axis is an index table, the B-axis does not rotate by manual operation.

                         (However, the axis can return to its home position in the manual position mode. Refer to
                         the "B-axis Rotation to the Home Position" in 25-3-4.)
                         The B-axis does not rotate by pulse handle feed, manual interruption, or pulse handle
                         overlap.

            (2) When the B-pallet is in the machine
                   The B-axis can rotate only in the range set at the system parameter B (B/R pallet).

                   Rotation range: N-limit of B/R pallet  B-axis machine coordinate  P-limit of B/R pallet

                   The other operations are the same with those executed when the A-pallet is in the machine.

            (3) When neither A-pallet nor B-pallet is in the machine
                   The B-axis can rotate only in the range between the travel limits set at the system parameter.

                   Rotation range: Negative travel limit  B-axis machine coordinate  Positive travel limit

                   The other operations are the same with those executed when the A-pallet is in the machine.

25-3-2.Multi-turn Axis Rotation Limit (Neither the 5th nor the 6th axis is
         connected and "1" is set at the NC optional parameter (bit) No.39,
         bit 4)

            The B-axis rotates as the multi-turn axis.
            [Refer to "Multi-turn Specification" in the Additional Axis (Rotary Axis) of the Special Functions
            Manual.]

            The limit inherent in each pallet is ignored.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-194
                                                                         SECTION 25 B-AXIS I/L WITH 2 ROTARY TABLES

25-3-3.Unlimited Axis Rotation Limit (Neither the 5th nor the 6th axis is
         connected, and "0" is set at the NC optional parameter (bit) No.39,
         bit 4)

            The B-axis rotates without any limit.

            The limit inherent in each pallet is ignored.

25-3-4.Note for B-axis Interlock

          Power ON/OFF and NC Reset

            (1) B-axis rotation limit (when 5th or 6th-axis or both are connected)
                    - When the NC is reset except the NC reset by machine lock ON/OFF, the current coordinate
                       is held without regard to the setting at the NC optional parameter (bit) No. 2, bit 1.
                    - When the NC is reset by power ON/OFF, machine lock ON/OFF, or for releasing an alarm A
                       (SVP alarm), the actual position is rounded off in the following manner.
                       <When A-pallet or B-pallet is in the machine>
                       The actual position is rounded off so that the B-axis is located in the rotation range of the
                       pallet in the machine.
                       If the actual position is outside the B-axis rotation range, the actual position is rounded off to
                       a nearby position in the rotation range.
                       <When neither A nor B pallet is in the machine>
                       The B-axis operates in the same way as the case where the power is turned ON/OFF or the
                       NC is reset for the multi-turn axis.
                       [Refer to "Multi-turn Specification" in Additional Axis (Rotary Axis) of the Special Functions
                       Manual.]

            (2) Multi-turn B-axis (Neither the 5th nor the 6th axis is connected and "1" is set at the NC optional
                   parameter (bit) No.39, bit 4)
                    - The B-axis operates in the same way as the case where the power is turned ON/OFF or the
                       NC is reset for the multi-turn axis.
                       [Refer to "Multi-turn Specification" in Additional Axis (Rotary Axis) of the Special Functions
                       Manual.]

            (3) Unlimited axis (Neither the 5th nor the 6th axis is connected, and "0" is set at the NC optional
                   parameter (bit) No.39, bit 4)
                    - Conforms to the specifications for the unlimited rotary axis.
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-195
                                                            SECTION 25 B-AXIS I/L WITH 2 ROTARY TABLES

B-axis Rotation to the Home Position

(1) B-axis rotation limit (when 5th or 6th axis or both are connected)
      The B-axis is positioned in its home position.
      The B-axis home position command values which are set on the home position screen
      displayed in the parameter setting mode are not rounded off to the range from 0.0000 to
      359.9999.

(2) Multi-turn axis (Neither 5th nor 6th axis is connected and "1" is set at the NC optional parameter
      (bit) No.39, bit 4.)
      The B-axis home position command values, which are set on the home position screen
      displayed in the parameter setting mode, are rounded off to the range from 0.0000 to 359.9999.

The B-axis rotates from the actual position to the position physically equal to the home position.
(This is a normal specification for the multi-turn axis.)

                        (C)

Actual position 0                 360      720  1080

Position (MC)

                   (B)       (A)

                                                ME32123R0702900080001

In this example, the home position is 0°.

(A) If the actual position is 240°
       The B-axis rotates clockwise from the actual position to the 360° position which is
       physically equal to the home position.

(B) If the actual position is 120°
       The B-axis rotates counterclockwise from the actual position to the 0° position.

(C) If the actual position is 180° (in the middle of 2 home positions), the B-axis rotates in the
       same manner as described in (A).

25-4. Multi-turn Axis Specifications

            (1) If the B-axis is an index table

                    Designation of a B-axis command with G31, G11, G236, or G92 results in the following

                         alarm:
                         2359 Alarm-B Data word: rotating axis command 2

                    Designation of a B-axis command with G02/G03 or G41/G42 results in the following alarm:

                         2359 Alarm-B Data word: rotating axis command 1

                    For the multi-turn B-axis, G91 can be specified with B-axis.

                    When G60 is specified with B-axis, the B-axis moves to the target position but the moving

                         direction is not unidirectional.

                    The B-axis is irrelevant to linear interpolation.

                    For the multi-turn B-axis, it rotates clockwise if its positional value increases, and rotates

                         counterclockwise if its positional value decreases.
                         For the B-axis which is not a multi-turn axis, its rotating direction conforms to the M15/16
                         command.

                    For the multi-turn B-axis, the mirror image is enabled by designation of G90/G91.

                    For the multi-turn B-axis, if the sequence restart is attempted, the B-axis position is

                         converted into a position where the B-axis can be returned by a rotation of at most 360°.
                         Then the axis is rotated to the restart position.

            (2) If the B-axis is not an index table

                    Designation of a B-axis command with G41, G42 results in the following alarm:

                         2359 Alarm-B Data word: rotating axis command 1

                    For multi-turn axis, G91 can be specified with B-axis.

                    When G60 is specified with B-axis, the B-axis is positioned in the target position by a

                         movement in one direction.

                    Linear interpolation is possible with the B-axis.

                    For the multi-turn B-axis, it rotates clockwise if its positional value increases, and rotates

                         counterclockwise if its positional value decreases.
                         For the B-axis which is not a multi-turn axis, its rotating direction conforms to the M15/16
                         command.

                    For the multi-turn B-axis, the mirror image is enabled by designation of G90/G91.

                    For the multi-turn B-axis, if the sequence restart is attempted, the B-axis position is

                         converted into a position where the B-axis can be returned by a rotation of at most 360°.
                         Then the axis is rotated to the restart position.

                                                                                                                                6103-E P-199
                                                                SECTION 26 B-AXIS ROTATION INTERLOCK FUNCTION

SECTION 26 B-AXIS ROTATION INTERLOCK FUNCTION

26-1. Overview

            Due to the pallet size and the shape of the fixture installed on the pallet, the spindle will interfere with
            the APC unit when the B-axis rotation is attempted near the positive Z-axis limit. When attempted
            near the negative Z-axis limit, the spindle interferes with the spindlehead.
            To prevent these interferences, the machine limits the Z-axis movable range in accordance with the
            B-axis angle. This is called B-axis interlock.

            The interlock is effective in all the operation modes (auto, MDI, and manual) and even in the
            machine lock mode.
                                                                                                              6103-E P-200
                                              SECTION 26 B-AXIS ROTATION INTERLOCK FUNCTION

                                                                                                 6103-E P-201
                                 SECTION 26 B-AXIS ROTATION INTERLOCK FUNCTION

26-3. Interference Check

When the B-axis rotation interlock is enabled, the interlock function prohibits the following
operations in all the operation modes to prevent interference. If disabled, no interlock is applied to
these operations.

26-3-1.Interlock in Automatic or MDI Operation

If the Z-axis is already located in a position above the Z-axis upper limit (B-axis rotation, Z-axis
upper limit is enabled) or below the Z-axis lower limit (B-axis rotation, Z-axis lower limit is enabled),
or the specified Z-axis command is larger than the Z-axis upper limit (B-axis rotation, Z-axis upper
limit is enabled) or smaller than the Z-axis lower limit (B-axis rotation, Z-axis lower limit is enabled),
the interlock function generates an alarm depending on the B-axis position.
Here, the actual position and the command position include the manual shift amount.

(1) Without B-axis command
      If the B-axis actual position is not located in any angle of B-axis rotation disable area, the
      interlock function generates an alarm B to stop the machine operation.

(2) With B-axis command
      If the B-axis is already located in any angle of B-axis rotation disable area and does not move,
      the machine generates an alarm B to stop the machine operation.

(3) When the Z-axis is already located above the Z-axis upper limit (B-axis rotation Z-axis upper
      limit is enabled) or below the Z-axis lower limit (B-axis rotation Z-axis lower limit is enabled), the
      Z-axis command for movement to the lower/upper limit range for B-axis rotation is effective as
      far as no B-axis command is issued. In this case, the interlock function generates no alarm
      regardless of the B-axis actual position.

The above interlock is also imposed on the movements to the home positions with M6 or G30.
To move the axes to their home positions without generating an alarm, determine the order of axis
movements so as not to trigger the above interlocks.

26-3-2.Interlock in Manual Feed

If you attempt to move the Z-axis to the outside of the Z-axis lower/upper limit range (when B-axis
rotation Z-axis upper/lower limit is enabled) for B-axis rotation by manual feed in the manual
interrupt or manual operation mode without locating the B-axis in any angle of B-axis rotation
disable area, the machine generates an alarm A to stop the machine.
The machine generates the alarm by judging the Z-axis command. Therefore, there are cases
where the actual position is not above the B-axis rotation area when the alarm A is reset. This is not
an abnormal case because motor operation delays after issue of the command.
This interlock is also imposed on the movements to the home positions by manual feed.

This interlock also checks the following commands and generates an alarm accordingly: pulse
handle overlap in automatic or MDI mode, a command of which axis movable range cannot be
judged only with the start and end points such as circular interpolation command and axis feed on a
slope.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-202
                                                                SECTION 26 B-AXIS ROTATION INTERLOCK FUNCTION

26-3-3.Interlock in Manual Feed Command

            (1) Z-axis movements
                   When the B-axis actual position (machine coordinate) is not located in any angle of B-axis
                   rotation disable area and the Z-axis actual position (machine coordinate) is outside the lower/
                   upper limit (when B-axis rotation Z-axis upper/lower limit is enabled), Z-axis rapid traverse,
                   cutting feed (JOG) or pulse handle feed in the direction in which the Z-axis will exceed the
                   range will cause an alarm B. In this case, the machine ignores the designated command.
                   If you attempt to move the Z-axis from the lower/upper limit range for B-axis rotation to the
                   outside, the interlock described in "26-3-2. Interlock in Manual Feed" operates to generate an
                   alarm A.
                     The directions in which the Z-axis will exceed the range are as follows:
                          Z-axis actual position is above the upper limit (when B-axis rotation Z-axis upper limit is
                          enabled)  Positive direction
                          Z-axis actual position is below the lower limit (when B-axis rotation Z-axis lower limit is
                          enabled)  Negative direction

            (2) B-axis movements
                   When the Z-axis actual position (machine coordinate) is outside the lower/upper limit range for
                   B-axis rotation (when B-axis rotation Z-axis upper/lower limit is enabled), designation of a B-
                   axis movement command such as B-axis manual feed, manual JOG feed, or pulse handle feed
                   causes an alarm B and the machine ignores the designated command.
                   On the machine with the indexable B-axis, however, only the rapid traverse is possible as far as
                   the NC specification code No.18, bit1 (index table manual operation) is effective.

SECTION 27 ATC SUB-OPERATION PANEL
                   SPECIFICATIONS

27-1. Overview

            The ATC sub-operation panel type allows users to operate tool supply and removal and tool data
            edit which are set on the MAGAZINE INFO sheet on the TOOL DATA screen. The sub-operation
            panel also has the function to automatically index the specified pot of the ATC magazine to the
            magazine tool change position. With this function, the setup time can be shortened by decreasing
            the movement between the proximate ATC magazine position and NC operation panel.
            The following figure shows the equipment configuration with the ATC sub operation panel. The
            actual configuration may differ from the figure below depending on the ATC specifications.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             ME32123R0703100010001

27-2. MG Manual Operation Panel

27-2-1.MG Manual Operation Panel: Standard Magazine and Multi-
         magazine

            (1) MG Manual Interrupt Switch

                                                                                                                                                       ME32123R0703100020001

                   The MG manual operation panel enters the MG manual interrupt state when the MG Manual
                   Interrupt switch is turned "ON" and the MG indexing is enabled.
            (2) MG Manual Interrupt LED
                   The MG manual interrupt LED turns ON when the MG manual operation panel enters the MG
                   manual interrupt state.
            (3) CW/ CCW Switch
                   In the MG manual interrupt state, depression of CW/CCW switch causes the MG to start
                   forward/reverse rotation.
            (4) Auto Index Switch
                   When the Auto Index switch is pressed in the MG manual interrupt state, the pot number
                   specified with the touch panel is indexed to the manual tool change pot position.

                    The input of the Auto Index switch is ignored while indexing forward or reverse rotation.
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-206
                                           SECTION 27 ATC SUB-OPERATION PANEL SPECIFICATIONS

        When the pot number specified with the touch panel is "0", the pot number is the same as

            the pot number indexed to the current manual tool change position or the pot number is
            larger than the maximum value for the pot number, the input of the Auto Index switch is
            ignored.

(5) MG Door Interlock LED
      When the MG Door Close LS is turned OFF while the ATC or MG is operating, the MG door
      interlock state alarm occurs and the MG Door Interlock LED lights.

(6) Restart SW
      This switch is used to release the MG door interlock state alarm and restart the ATC operation.

(7) MG Selection LED
      The LED lights to indicate the selected magazine according to the pot number specified with the
      touch panel.

27-2-2.MG Manual Operation: Matrix Magazine (MA-400H)

            (1) MG Manual Interrupt Switch
                   The MG manual operation panel enters the MG manual interrupt state when the MG manual
                   interrupt switch is turned ON and the call and storage operations are enabled.

            (2) MG Manual Interrupt LED
                   The MG manual interrupt LED turns ON when the MG manual operation panel enters the MG
                   manual interrupt state.

            (3) Call LED
                   This LED lights up when the Call button is effective and blinks when the call operation starts.

            (4) Call Button
                   Press this button to transfer the pot on the selected tool rack location to the manual tool change
                   position (call operation).

            (5) Store LED
                   This LED lights up when the Store button is effective and blinks when the storage operation
                   starts.

            (6) Store Button
                   Press this button to store the pot that has been called to the manual tool change position back
                   onto the original tool rack location (storage operation).

            (7) Door Interlock LED
                   When the MG door close confirmation switch is turned OFF during the ATC operation and call/
                   storage operation, the machine enters the MG door interlock state and the MG door interlock
                   LED lights up.

            (8) Restart SW
                   This switch is used to release the MG door interlock state alarm and restart the ATC operation.

                                                                                                                                                                                                                      ME32123R0703100030001
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-208
                                                        SECTION 27 ATC SUB-OPERATION PANEL SPECIFICATIONS

27-2-3.MG Manual Operation: Matrix Magazine (with Gripper for Next
         Tool Preparation)

            (1) MG MANUAL INT. Switch
                   When the MG MANUAL INT. switch is turned to ON, the MG manual interrupt state is
                   established, enabling call and storage operations.

            (2) MG MANUAL INT. LED
                   The MG MANUAL INT. LED turns ON when the MG manual interrupt state is established.

            (3) CALL LED
                   This LED is lit when the CALL button is enabled and blinks when the call operation starts.

            (4) CALL Button
                   When this button is pressed, the pot on the selected tool rack location is transferred to the
                   manual tool change position (call operation).

            (5) STORAGE LED
                   This LED is lit when the STORAGE button is enabled and blinks when the storage operation
                   starts.

            (6) STORAGE Button
                   When this button is pressed, the pot that has been called to the manual tool change position is
                   stored in the original tool rack (storage operation).

            (7) DOOR INTERLOCK LED
                   When the MG door close confirmation signal is turned to OFF during ATC, call or storage
                   operation, the machine enters the MG door interlock state and the MG DOOR INTERLOCK
                   LED will light up.

            (8) RESTART Button
                   This button is used to release the MG door interlock state and restart the operation.

            (9) NEXT TOOL RETURN LED
                   This LED is lit when the NEXT TOOL RETURN button is enabled and blinks when the next tool
                   return operation starts.

            (10) NEXT TOOL RETURN Button
                   This button is used to return the next tool that has been prepared at the tool transfer position for
                   the machine back to the original tool rack.

            (11) NEXT TOOL RE-PREPARATION LED
                   This LED is lit when the NEXT TOOL RE-PREPARATION button is enabled and blinks when
                   the next tool re-preparation operation starts.

            (12) NEXT TOOL RE-PREPARATION BUTTON
                   This button is used to transfer the tool that is returned to the tool rack by the NEXT TOOL
                   RETURN button to the tool transfer position for the machine.

            (13) NG TOOL LED (1 to 4)
                   When the NG tool is in the NG tool buffer ST, the NG tool buffer ST no. of the NG tool is lit. The
                   LED will blink when the NG tool is collected, and will go off when NG TOOL COLLECT
                   CONFIRMATION button is pressed after the NG tool has been collected.

            (14) NG TOOL COLLECT CONFIRMATION Button
                   Press this button when the NG tool has been collected from the NG tool buffer ST. Then the NG
                   tool collection is confirmed, the NG tool buffer ST data are cleared.
