27-3. Manual Magazine Operation

27-3-1.Magazine Indexing (Standard Magazine and Multi-magazine)

(1) Normal Rotation/ Reverse Rotation Index
      The MG starts indexing at the rising edge of the CW/CCW switch signal and positions the
      proximate pot at the falling edge of the signal.
      The input of the Auto Index switch is ignored while indexing normal rotation or reverse rotation.

(2) Automatic Indexing
      When the Auto Index switch is turned ON, the MG indexes the pot of which number is specified
      with the touch panel to the manual tool change position.

(3) Manual Tool Change Position

     Parameters

 MACHINE AXIS PARAMETER(MA) ZERO OFFSET: Used to position the MG by the ATC

   command.

 MACHINE USER PARAMETER No.7-2 18: Used to position the pot for manual tool

   change.

MACHINE USER PARAMETER No. 7-2 ATC/ TOOL EX CHANGE

    TOOL EX CHANGE POSITION

    Command: Setting

18  Default: 0
    Minimum Value: 0

    Maximum Value: Number of pots in one matrix of the magazine -1

    Unit: None

This parameter is used to determine the pot position for manual tool change by counting the number
of pots from the ATC tool change pot position to the manual tool change pot position in the MG
reverse rotating direction.
The manual tool change pot position is common to all the magazines.

Example) In the example below, the pot number 16 is set as the pot position for manual tool

27-4. Touch Panel

27-4-1.Window Configuration

            The touch panel has a MENU window and the other five windows, which transit alternately with the
            MENU window.
            The detailed information of each window is to be described later.

             0: MENU Window
             1: TOOL/ POT INDEX Window
             2: NG TOOL INDEX Window
             3: POT & TOOL NO. TABLE window
             4: TOOL DATA SET UP Window
             5: NG TOOL BUFFER ST Window (This window may not be displayed depending on the MG

                     specification.)

The "4: TOOL DATA SET UP Window" comprise the following five pages.

4-1: TOOL DATA SET UP-1 (pot no., tool no., group no.)
4-2: TOOL DATA SET UP-1 (1st tool length offset amount) (1st tool length-shape amount/1st tool

        length-wear amount, 1st tool radius-shape amount/1st tool radius-wear amount) [*]
4-3: TOOL DATA SET UP-2 (2nd tool length offset amount) (2nd tool length-shape amount/2nd

        tool length-wear amount, 2nd tool radius-shape amount/2nd tool radius-wear amount) [*]
4-4: TOOL DATA SET UP-3 (3rd tool length offset amount) (3rd tool length-shape amount/3rd tool

        length-wear amount, 3rd tool radius-shape amount/3rd tool radius-wear amount) [*]
4-5: TOOL DATA SET UP-5 (tool management mode, tool life, status [OK/NG])
[*] When the tool wear offset function is provided.

      Lights up when an error occurs during touch panel operation.

(3) Error No. Display
      Displays the error number when an error occurs during touch panel operation. (For the detail of
      errors, refer to "27-5.Error List.")

(4) ER.CLR Button
      Clears an error caused by an erroneous touch panel operation.
      The ERROR Lamp will go off and the error number display is cleared to 0000.

(5) MENU Button
      The screen transits to the MENU window.

(6) MGNo.
      Displays the MG number.
      The MG number to be automatically indexed will light up.

(7) POT No.
      Displays the number of the pot located in the manual tool change pot position of each
      magazine.
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-215
                                           SECTION 27 ATC SUB-OPERATION PANEL SPECIFICATIONS

(8) TOOL No.
      Displays the number of the tool located in the manual tool change pot position of each
      magazine. When no tool has been set, "**" is displayed.

(9) INDEX POT No.
      Displays the number of pot indexed automatically. The same value remains on the window until
      the next index command is newly specified.

(10) Input Pallet
      The method to input characters and numbers using the touch panel can be switched with the
      circle-shaped switches located at the top of the window.
      The number pallet appears when the Menu window is displayed. The alphabet (1) (A to M)
      pallet is displayed by pressing the "A-M" key. The alphabet (2) (N to Z) pallet is displayed by
      pressing the "N-Z" key.

Number pallet  Alphabet (1) (A to M) pallet  Alphabet (2) (N to Z) pallet

                                                                                                                                                                                                               ME32123R0703100080002

(11) Entry Column
      Displays the character strings entered from the input pallet in the bottom entry column. The set
      value appears in the top column after being confirmed by pressing the "Enter" key.
      When the window is changed, the entry column at the bottom is cleared.

(12) POT SELECT and TOOL SELECT Buttons
      Selects whether the pot number or the tool number is used for automatic index specification.
      The status last set is retained when the power is cycled.

POT & TOOL No. TABLE Window

Displays the table of pot numbers and tool numbers.
Ten tools in pot appear on each page.
The pot numbers and tool numbers can be searched by specifying the number.

                                                                                 (3)

(1)  (2)

                                              (4)

                                                                                                                                                                               ME32123R0703100100001

(1) POT No.
      Displays the ten pot numbers from the top of the pot and tool no. table.
      Only the pot numbers that can be set will be displayed.
      When the number of the pots to be displayed in the page is less than ten, "0" is displayed.

(2) TOOL No.
      Displays the corresponding tool numbers of the pot numbers.
      When no tool has been set, "**" is displayed.
      When the number of the pots to be displayed in the page is less than ten, it is shown as blank.

(3) P  Button (previous page) /P  (next page)
      Displays the ten pot numbers (and the tool numbers) from the top of the pot and tool no. table.
      Previous ten or next ten numbers are displayed on the page by pressing P  Button (previous
      page) or P  (next page) accordingly.

(4) POT SEARCH Button /TOOL SEARCH Button
      Specify either the pot number or tool number for the data to be displayed.
      The status last set is retained when the power is cycled.
      Either the pot or tool number is searched for from the pot and tool no. table according to the
      SEARCH Button, and the display data are updated by placing the found pot at the top.

                                           SECTION 27 ATC SUB-OPERATION PANEL SPECIFICATIONS

TOOL DATA SET UP-1 Window (pot no., tool no., group no.)

This window displays and sets the data of the tools stored in the pot.
In this window the tool length offset value can be set in the same way done in the tool data setting
mode. The pot can be selected along the (magazine) forward rotation (ascending or descending
order) or by directly entering the pot number.
The tool data will be updated at the time when the window is switched and the data changed from
the operation panel are also reflected.
The data of pot number 1 are displayed when the window is switched to this window from another
window. When the automatic index command is specified, however, the data of the pot number with
which the automatic index is performed are displayed.

(2)  (3)       (1)

(4)       (5)

(6)  (7)

                                                 (8)

                                                                                                                                                                                ME32123R0703100110001

(1) P  Button (previous page) /P  (next page)
      Switches the pages. By pressing the next page button the TOOL DATA SET UP-2 window is
      displayed. By pressing the previous page button the TOOL DATA SET UP-5 window is
      displayed.

(2) POT No. Button
      When the Enter key is pressed with the "pot number setting button" displayed in highlight, the
      "tool number" and "tool group" of the tool data of the pot number set in the input column are
      displayed.
      If this is set when the current tool number is being displayed, error number "0002" appears.

(3) POT No.
      Displays the pot number currently selected.

(4) TOOL No. Button
      When the Enter key is pressed with "the tool number setting button" displayed in highlight, the
      OSP in the machine finds the same tool number as the value in the input column from the
      registered tool data list (which is on the left side of the TOOL DATA sheet in the TOOL DATA
      screen) to apply the tool number and the tool information to the pot currently displayed. (This is
      the tool supply operation when the "tool supply" function key is pressed on the machine OSP
      side.
      In input operation, only the tool number is to be entered for tool specification. On the screen,
      however, the tool number and tool attribution are displayed.
                                                                                     6103-E P-221
              SECTION 27 ATC SUB-OPERATION PANEL SPECIFICATIONS

Setting "*" for a tool number means "no tool". Setting "D" for a tool number means "dummy tool".
If the attribution is set when the current tool number is being displayed, error number "0002"
appears.
The tool attribution to be displayed is as in the following table.

        Kind                                         Detail          Setting value

              Planer tool                                            [P]

              Attachment tool                                        [A]

              U-axis tool                                            [U]

              Manual attachment tool                                 [N]

Diameter      Plug tool                                              [B]
              Through tool                                           [C]

              Turning tool                                           [T]

              Grinding tool                                          [G]

              Probe                                                  [Q]

              Reference tool                                         [Z]

Weight        Heavy tool                                             [M]
              Light tool                                             [S]

length        Long tool                                              [E]

              Both large diameter tool                               [L]

              Super large diameter tool                              [F]

              Small diameter tool                                    [H]

Tool          Standard boring bar                                    [R]
              Medium diameter tool                                   [J]

              Medium diameter boring bar                             [K]

              Large diameter boring bar with single tip              [V]

              Large diameter boring bar with double tips             [W]

(5) Tool Number
      Displays the tool number currently selected.
      When no tool has been set, "**" is displayed.

(6) GROUP No. Button
      When the Enter key is pressed with the "tool group setting button" displayed in highlight, the
      value in the input column is set to the tool group of the pot currently displayed.
      If this is set when the current tool number is being displayed, error number "0002" appears.

(7) Tool Group
      Displays the tool group of the pot number currently selected.
      When no tool has been set, "0" is displayed.

(8) POT  Button (previous pot) / POT (next pot)
      Switch the pots. By pressing the next pot button the pot data with which "1" is added to the pot
      number currently displayed is displayed. By pressing the previous pot button the pot data with
      which "1" is subtracted from the pot number currently displayed is displayed.

Refer to aforementioned "TOOL/POT INDEX Window" for the description about following items:
Window name, ERROR Lamp, Error No. Display, ER.CLR Button, MENU Button, and Input Pallet
and Entry Column.

TOOL DATA SET UP-5 Window (tool management mode, tool life, status [OK/
NG])

This window displays and sets the data of the tools stored in the pot.
In this window the items regarding tool life data are set and displayed.
Moreover, NG tools are reset by the NG RESET button (setting the left tool life to the same value as
the tool life, setting the tool status to OK.)

(1)                      (2)

(3)            (4)

(6)            (7)       (5)

                    (8)

                                      ME32123R0703100240001

(1) MNG. MODE Button
      When the Enter key is pressed with the MNG. MODE button displayed in highlight, the value in
      the input column is set to the tool management mode of the pot currently displayed. The tool
      management mode has the following kinds. Specify the mode with the number.

Setting value                         Display
        0
        1      NO
        3      TIME (Changeover)
        4      TIME
        6      NUMBER (Changeover)
        7      NUMBER
        9      DISTANCE (Changeover)
               DISTANCE

       * DISTANCE (Changeover) and DISTANCE can be set and displayed when bit 0 of the NC
            optional parameter bit No.87 is set to ON.

      If this is set when no tool has been mounted, error number "0001" appears.
      If this is set when the current tool number is being displayed, error number "0002" appears.

(2) Tool management mode
      The tool management mode of the pot number currently selected is displayed.

(3) LIFE SET Button
      When the Enter key is pressed with the LIFE SET button displayed in highlight, the value in the
      input column is set to the tool life and to the left tool life of the pot currently displayed.
      If this is set when no tool has been mounted, error number "0001" appears.
      If this is set when the current tool number is being displayed, error number "0002" appears.
                                                                                      6103-E P-228
               SECTION 27 ATC SUB-OPERATION PANEL SPECIFICATIONS

(4) Tool life
      The tool life (time, number, distance) of the pot number currently selected is displayed.
      When the management mode is set with time (min) and distance (m), the value is displayed as
      an integer by cutting off after the decimal point.
      When no tool has been set, "0" is displayed.

(5) LEFT
      The left tool life (time, number, distance) of the pot number currently selected is displayed.
      When the management mode is set with time (min) and distance (m), the value is displayed as
      an integer by cutting off after the decimal point.
      When no tool has been set, "0" is displayed.

(6) STATUS Button
      When the Enter key is pressed with the STATUS button displayed in highlight, the value in the
      input column is set to the tool status of the pot currently displayed. When "1 (OK)" is set, the
      setting value of tool life is also set to the left value as the initial value.
      The tool status that can be set here is either "1 (OK)" or "3 (NG2)" only.
      If this is set when no tool has been mounted, error number "0001" appears.
      If this is set when the current tool number is being displayed, error number "0002" appears.

(7) Tool status
      The tool status of the pot number currently selected is displayed.
      The character-strings that have been registered in the character-string table in the touch panel
      of the value set by the NC software are displayed.

Setting value                           Display
        1
        2      OK
        3      NG1
        4      NG2
               NG3

(8) NG RESET Button
      With the currently selected pot:

 Press NG RESET button with the NG tool mounted.

    After the setting value of tool life of the target tool is set to the left tool life value, the tool
   status becomes OK.

 Press NG RESET button with the OK tool mounted.

    No operation is performed.

 Press NG RESET button with no tool mounted.

    Error number "0001" appears.
   When the current tool number is being displayed, error number "0002" appears when the
   button is pressed.

Error No.                                      Description                           Registered Error
   0001                                                                                    Message
   0002    The selected pot has no tool.
           No tool data can be set because the active tool number is selected  Tool none
   8201    at the TOOL No.
   8202    The entered value is out of the setting range.                      Input data
           The entered tool has already been registered in the Pot & Tool No.  Registered tool
   8203    Table.

           The setting of large-diameter tool can be cancelled because the     Can not clear tool No.
   8210    adjacent pots are not open.
   8215    The tool cannot be set since the machine is operating.              Parameter interlock

27-5-2.Matrix Magazine Errors Related to Call or Storage Command

Error No.                                                         Description
   1810    A call/storage command is issued during an ATC cycle.
   1811    A call/storage command is issued with an ATC sequence number other than 1 and 12.
   1812    A non-existing tool rack number is specified by a call command.
   1813    A tool rack number with no pot is specified by a call command.
   1814    A call command is issued with a pot at the manual change position.
   1815    A tool rack number to which a safety tool has been assigned is specified by a call command.
   1816    The tool rack number to which the active tool has been assigned is specified by a call
           command.
   1817    The tool rack number to which the next tool has been assigned is specified by a call command.
   1818    A storage command is issued with a pot at the specified tool rack location.
   1819    A storage command is issued with no pot at the manual tool change position.
   1820    A storage command is issued with non-safety tools before and after the specified tool rack
           location if the tool to be stored is a large-diameter one.
   1821    A call/storage command is issued with the manual change lever not returned.

27-6. Input/Output Bit

27-6-1.Input Bit

          Standard Magazine and Multi-magazine

   Label                                     Comment
iMGIN     1st operation panel Manual Interrupt SW
iMGCW     1st operation panel CW SW
iMGCC     1st operation panel CCW SW
iMRST     1st operation panel Restart SW

                                                 SECTION 28 TOOL BREAKAGE NO-LOAD DETECTION FUNCTION

SECTION 28 TOOL BREAKAGE NO-LOAD DETECTION
                   FUNCTION

28-1. Overview

            This function simply detects the no-load cutting status of a spindle caused by tool breakage and
            stops the operation by issuing an alarm.

28-2. Details

            You can set 80 values (1 to 80) to detect the no-load cutting status of a spindle.
            A system variable in a command determines the value to use for the no-load monitoring. NC
            resetting does not clear this value. The value is also kept when the power is turned on or off.
            When the system value is 0, the no-load monitoring is not performed.
            The no-load monitoring of a spindle is enabled by M codes. There are two M codes that enable the
            monitoring. One enables the monitoring for the command block only, and the other one enables the
            monitoring from an M command to another M command.
            If the detected value does not exceed the above pre-set value during the no-load monitoring while
            this function is enabled, an alarm is issued and the operation is stopped. You can choose the alarm
            level from A, B or C.

            A fixed cycle automatically generates a slip block, which does not accept the above M-codes. An M
            code determines whether or not to monitor no-load status for the spindle at the cutting block. This M
            command is a modal command.

            This function can automatically determines whether the spindle is accelerating or decelerating, and
            disable the no-load monitoring during the acceleration or deceleration by parameter setting.
              The above pre-set value can be changed to "the value for the rated torque" or "the value that

                  supports the attachment load display" by setting the bit 4 of the NC optional parameter bit No. 68.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-235
                                                 SECTION 28 TOOL BREAKAGE NO-LOAD DETECTION FUNCTION

            Set the parameters for this function on the "spindle no-load monitor parameter" screen.

28-2-2.System Variables

            (1) No-load monitoring parameter number
                   VNLDN
                   Value range: 0 to 80
                   The variable specifies the no-load monitoring parameter number used for the no-load
                   monitoring function. It is possible to load a value for the variable.
                   VNLDN is backed up. If the value is not specified, the previously used value is set.
                   When 0 is set, the no-load monitoring is disabled.
                   0 is set by default at factory.

            (2) No-load monitoring value
                   VNLDT[, ]
                    = 1 to 80 No-load monitoring parameter No.
                    = 1, 2, 3 No-load monitoring parameter type
                        1: No-load monitoring value
                        2: Continuous monitoring time
                        3: Alarm level
                   It enables reading or writing of the type of the set value indicated by  for the no-load monitoring
                   parameter number indicated by .

                   Value range:
                   When  = 1: no-load monitoring value: 0 to 200
                   When  = 2: Continuous monitoring time: 0 to 200
                   When  = 3: Alarm level: 0 to 2 (0 = alarm A, 1= alarm B, 2 = alarm C)
                                                                                                  6103-E P-238
                   SECTION 28 TOOL BREAKAGE NO-LOAD DETECTION FUNCTION

28-2-3.M Commands

The M codes turn on and off functions.
There are three types of M codes as follows.

(1) The M code to perform simple no-load monitoring for the command block only

(2) The M code to perform simple no-load monitoring from the start command block to the end
      command block

(3) The M code to determine whether or not to perform simple no-load monitoring for a fixed cycle

These M commands are enabled for the machine lock and STM lock.

The M Code to Perform Simple No-load Monitoring for the Command Block
Only

Simple no-load monitoring is performed in the command block only.
M917: Simple no-load monitoring enabled block

The command is issued to the block to perform the simple no-load monitoring at the same time. It is
used for drilling without a fixed cycle.
If the detected spindle load does not exceed the no-load value in the block (from the beginning to
the end of the block) where simple no-load monitoring is enabled, it is regarded that the no-load
status occurred at the spindle accompanied by tool breakage. An alarm is also issued in this case.
If M 917 is issued for continuous blocks, the process is performed for each block.
If the block where M917 is issued is not feeding (G0 or G4), the simple no-load monitoring is still
performed.
The M code is a one-shot M command. It is enabled for one block only. The group number is 46.

Program example)         ; no-load monitoring parameter number setting
      OHOLE
      VNLDN=PDN          ; It performs simple no-load monitoring with the no-load monitoring
      G0 X=PX Y=PY Z=PR    parameter PDN.
      G1 Z=PZ M917 F=PF

      G0 Z=PR
      RTS
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-239
                                    SECTION 28 TOOL BREAKAGE NO-LOAD DETECTION FUNCTION

The M Code to Perform Simple No-load Monitoring from the Start Command
Block to the End Command Block

It specifies "ON" or "OFF" for the simple no-load monitoring.
    M923: Simple no-load monitoring "ON"
    M924: Simple no-load monitoring "OFF"

To perform simple no-load monitoring to multiple blocks, issue M924 to the start block and M923 for
the end block.
If the detected spindle load does not exceed the no-load value between M924 (the starting block)
and M923 (ending block), it is regarded that the no-load status occurred at the spindle accompanied
by tool breakage. An alarm is also issued. If the blocks where M924 and M923 are issued are not
feeding (G0 or G4), the simple no-load monitoring is still performed.
M917 (simple no-load monitoring enabled block command) issued to any of these blocks is ignored.
These M codes are modal M commands. The group number is 63.
M923 is issued when the power is turned on or NC is reset (including mode reset).

The M Code to Determine Whether or not to Perform Simple No-load
Monitoring for a Fixed Cycle

It turns on or off the simple no-load monitoring in cutting in a fixed cycle.
    M918 turns off the simple no-load monitoring in a fixed cycle.
    M919 turns on the simple no-load monitoring in a fixed cycle.

To perform the simple no-load monitoring in cutting in a fixed cycle, issue M919 before the fixed
cycle command, and issue M918 in the block where you do not need to perform the monitoring.
When the simple no-load monitoring is on and the detected spindle load does not exceed the no-
load value in cutting in a fixed cycle, it is regarded that a no-load status occurred at the spindle
accompanied by the tool breakage. An alarm is issued in this case.
These M codes are modal M commands.
M918 is issued when the power is turned on or NC is reset (including mode reset).

Refer to 28-2-5. "Fixed Cycle" for the details of the simple no-load monitoring block in a fixed cycle.
M 924 is automatically generated internally at the cutting start block and M923 is automatically
generated internally in the cutting end block in a fixed cycle.
If an M924 is issued in this manner and another M924 has been already issued, the previously
issued M924 is given a priority and monitoring continues by it. M923 is not generated for the cutting
end block in this case.

Program example)  ; no-load monitoring parameter number setting
      G00X_Y_     ; turns on the simple no-load monitoring in a fixed cycle.
      VNLDN=1     ; performs simple no-load monitoring with the no-load monitoring
      M919
      G81           parameter 1.
                  ; no-load monitoring parameter number setting
      VNLDN=2     ; performs simple no-load monitoring with the no-load monitoring
      G85
                    parameter 2.
      M918        ; turns off the simple no-load monitoring in a fixed cycle.
      G84         ; the simple no-load monitoring is not performed.
      G80
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-240
                                    SECTION 28 TOOL BREAKAGE NO-LOAD DETECTION FUNCTION

No-load Monitoring Enabled Block in the Cutter Radius Compensation

This subsection explains the details of turning on or off the simple no-load monitoring during the
cutter radius compensation.

(1) At the Time of the Radius Compensation Approach

        When the following M codes are issued at the time of the radius compensation approach at

            the beginning of the cutter radius compensation
            M924: enabled from the start point
            M917: enabled from the start point to the single block stop point
            M923: enabled to the single block stop point

(2) At the Time of External Cutting (0°   < 180°)

        When the intersecting angle between the move command where the M code is and the

            previous move command is 1°   < 180°
            M924: enabled from the single block stop point
            M917: enabled from the single block stop point to the point where the rectilinear travel to
            the first compensation point finishes
            M923: enabled to the point where the rectilinear travel to the first compensation point
            finishes

        When the intersecting angle between the move command where the M code is and the

            previous move command is  = 0°
            M924: enabled from the single block stop point
            M917: enabled from the current single block stop point to the single block stop point in the
            next move command
            M923: enabled to the single block stop point in the next move command

(3) At the Time of Internal Cutting (180°  )

        When the move command where the M code is and the previous move command are

            performing internal cutting
            M924: enabled from the single block stop point
            M917: enabled from the current single block stop point to the single block stop point in the
            next move command
            M923: enabled to the single block stop point in the next move command
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-241
                                                 SECTION 28 TOOL BREAKAGE NO-LOAD DETECTION FUNCTION

28-2-4.Spindle No-load Monitoring Methods

          Spindle No-load Monitoring Methods

If the spindle load keeps exceeding the "no-load value" while the no-load monitoring is on, and the
time of the occurrence does not exceed the "continuous monitoring time", an alarm is issued at the
specified level.

 The meaning of the "no-load value" against the spindle load varies depending on the status of
    the NC optional parameter bit No. 68 bit4 as follows.

No.  bit                                         Description

68   4              The set value in the "load maximum (%)" for the simple load
                    monitoring and no-load monitoring of the load display function for
                    each attachment is used for the ratio against the rating torque
                    command value.

                    The set value in the "load maximum (%)" for the simple load
                    monitoring and no-load monitoring of the load display function for
                    each attachment is used for the ratio according to the attachment
                    load display.

Spindle Load Value

The load value used for the spindle no-load monitoring is a simple average calculated using the
"average number of spindle load" parameter among the spindle no-load monitor parameters.
If 0 is set in the "average number of spindle load" parameter, the spindle load value is used as is.

Spindle No-load Monitoring Ignoring Conditions

A spindle experiences increased load during accelerating and decelerating. This function prevents
the machine from recognizing this increase of the load as the machining load. This function is not
required to perform the spindle no-load monitoring after the spindle revolutions are stabilized.
It is used when the new parameter "Spindle accel./decal. Load ignore" is enabled in the "spindle no-
load monitoring".

When the Machining is Not Synchronized Tapping

   When a command specifies the increased number of the spindle revolutions

      After the actual revolutions increase and reach the "spindle accelerating/decelerating
      determining revolutions (%)" and until the "spindle accelerating/decelerating wait time" elapses,
      it is regarded that there is no load. The spindle no-load monitoring is ignored.

   When a command specifies the decreased number of the spindle revolutions

      After the actual revolutions decrease and reach the "spindle accelerating/decelerating
      determining revolutions (%)" and until the "spindle accelerating/decelerating wait time" elapses,
      it is regarded that there is no load. The spindle no-load monitoring is ignored.

If 0 is set for the "spindle accelerating/decelerating determining revolutions (%)", the above
procedures are not performed.

When the commanded revolutions are 0 (spindle stop), the monitoring is ignored immediately and it
is regarded that there is no load.
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-242
                                    SECTION 28 TOOL BREAKAGE NO-LOAD DETECTION FUNCTION

If the spindle is at a complete stop and the spindle servo is off, including when the spindle is not in
the speed control mode, the monitoring is ignored and it is regarded that there is no load.

                                    Spindle accelerating/decelerating monitoring

                Spindle load

Spindle                              Time
revolutions  Spindle accelerating/decelerating wait time

                                                S command
                     Spindle accelerating/decelerating
                     determining revolutions

                                                                                   Time

                                                                                                                                                                               ME32123R0703200120001

           Fig.28-4 Period of time when the spindle no-load monitoring is ignored on start-up

When the Machining is Synchronized Tapping

After the actual spindle revolutions increase and reach the "spindle accelerating/decelerating
determining revolutions (%)" and until the spindle finishes accelerating, it is regarded that there is no
load. The spindle no-load monitoring is ignored.
When the spindle start decelerating, the no-load monitoring is ignored immediately and it is
regarded that there is no load.

28-2-5.Fixed Cycle

The simple no-load monitoring starts and ends in a fixed cycle as follows.
If the load detected from the Point R to the Point Z exceeds the no-load value, it is regarded that
there is no breakage.
The monitoring is performed for the behavior that goes back to the start point by rapid traverse for
repetitive cutting.

                   SETTING IN PARAMETER

29-1. Overview

The rotary axis of both with limitation and without limitation (normal one) can be switched by setting
the value of NC optional parameter bit when turning the power on. Use the NC optional parameter
bit No.70 bit 6.

NC optional parameter  Status  Description
              bit

No.70 bit6                     Select limited rotary axis at power-on.
                               Select normal rotary axis at power-on.

This parameter becomes effective when the power is turned on.

 This function is effective when the forth axis name is "A" by the factory setting and the machine has
    no limitation specification.

SECTION 30 RE-CALCULATING THE COORDINATE
                   COMMAND FUNCTION

30-1. Overview

            The G90(absolute command) was necessary after commanding home positioning (G30) or
            changing the coordinate system (G15,G16 and G92, etc.) to position each axis (establishing the
            coordinate). Re-calculating the coordinate function eliminates the need of G90.

             [Command format]
                  G97

             [Supplement]
                  The G97 is effective only for one block.
                  The G97 cannot be commanded with axis movement command at the same time. If done, an
                  alarm will occur.

                                                                                                                                6103-E P-260
                                                                                     SECTION 32 OIL MIST CYCLE FUNCTION

SECTION 32 OIL MIST CYCLE FUNCTION

32-1. Overview

            The oil mist discharge during the fixed cycle is usually performed during the cutting feed. This
            function allows the oil mist discharge at the timing of R-point travel during the fixed cycle (G84,
            G282, G283, G284, G74, G272, G273, G274, G85, G89).

32-2. Function

32-2-1.Oil mist cycle mode ON/OFF

            (1) Oil mist cycle mode ON
                    - M90 : Oil mist cycle mode ON

            (2) Oil mist cycle mode OFF
                   The oil mist cycle mode is turned to OFF with the command below.
                    - M09 : Coolant group OFF
                    - M07 : Oil mist coolant ON
                                   Although the oil mist cycle mode is turned to OFF, oil mist is sprayed by the
                                   M07 command.
                    - Oil mist ON command by depression of the button
                                 : Although the oil mist cycle mode is turned to OFF, oil mist is sprayed by the
                                   depression of the button.
                    - Power ON/OFF

            [Supplement]

              The oil mist cycle mode is not turned to OFF with the reset button or mode reset.

32-2-2.Detail

            (1) When the following fixed cycle command is issued with the oil mist cycle mode ON, the oil mist
                   is sprayed when traveling across the R-point for the duration set in the parameter.
                    - G74 : Reverse tapping cycle
                    - G84 : Tapping cycle
                    - G85 : Boring cycle
                    - G89 : Boring cycle
                    - G272 : Deep hole synchronous tapping, reverse tapping cycle
                    - G273 : Hi-speed deep hole synchronous tapping, reverse tapping cycle
                    - G274 : Synchronous tapping, tapping cycle
                    - G282 : Deep hole synchronous tapping, tapping cycle
                    - G283 : Hi-speed deep hole synchronous tapping, tapping cycle
                    - G284 : Synchronous tapping, tapping cycle

            (2) When the M87 (oil mist spray) command is issued with the oil mist cycle mode ON, the oil mist
                   is sprayed for the duration set in the parameter.
                                                                            6103-E P-261
                                 SECTION 32 OIL MIST CYCLE FUNCTION

 Oil mist is sprayed for the duration set in the following parameter.

   Set the parameter in the MACHINE USER PARAMETER screen (Coolant) parameter setting
   mode.

SECTION 33 MULTI-LANGUAGE SPECIFICATION

33-1. Overview

            This section describes the switching language procedure for the display message in OSP-P series.

33-2. Switching Language Procedure

            Procedure :

           1 Turn on the power to launch the NC.
           2 Press the "Environmental Setting" key among the "vertical function" keys shown

                   in the right of the window.

3 Press the "Language Change" key appeared in the "vertical function" keys.  E32123R0703800020001

                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               3R0703800020002

  4 Locate the cursor to the desired language in the window for switching language appeared in

      the center of the window. Then press "OK" in this window. The window will disappear soon.

  5 Turn on the power again. The NC will be launched in the desired language.

That is the end of operation.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-263
                                                                                SECTION 34 WORKPIECE WEIGHT SETTING

SECTION 34 WORKPIECE WEIGHT SETTING

34-1. Overview

            Workpiece weight selection function (servo file selection function) is to set workpiece weight (load)
            and have the servo file which contains optimal servo motor data automatically selected. NC selects
            optimal servo motor data from the workpiece weight set at the WORK WEIGHT SET when it is
            booted or reset.

            : WORK WEIGHT SET (unit: ton or 100 kg (220 lb)) is on the WORK WEIGHT SET screen under
            the PARAMETER screen.

34-2. Function

34-2-1.Workpiece Weight Setting (Rebooting the NC is not required)

   No alarm message appears if optimal servo motor data do not differ between before1 and after the
   workpiece weight setting is made. Moreover, NC utilizes the data in the WORK WEIGHT SET also
   for deflection compensation when the deflection compensation function is valid. Accordingly,
   workpiece weight is switched on line.

34-2-2.Workpiece Weight Setting (Resetting or rebooting the NC is
         required)

   Alarm message, "4336 Alarm D Reset effective parameter set" appears if optimal servo motor data
   differs between before and after the workpiece weight setting is made2.
   Value set for the workpiece weight does not become valid until the NC is reset or rebooted.
   (Servo motor data remains as is until they are updated by resetting or rebooting the NC.)

                          Workpiece weight set
                          when NC is booted

0                      A                        B   C                      Workpiece weight

*2:                       *1:                       *2:                    Servo motor

weight cannot be          weight can be modified    weight cannot be
modified unless the       without rebooting the     modified unless the                     ME32123R0703900030001
NC is reset.              NC since weight variance  NC is reset.
                          is covered by deflection
                          compensation function.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-264
                                                                                SECTION 34 WORKPIECE WEIGHT SETTING

34-2-3.BRAKE ON TIME and BRAKE OFF TIME

            If the shaft subject to the workpiece weight travels toward weight vector, it may fall off during
            changing servo motor data.
            To avoid this error, brake is applied to the motor before and after the servo motor data is updated.
            Time the brake is applied is set at BRAKE ON TIME and BRAKE OFF TIME on the 2nd page of
            WORK WEIGHT SET screen.
            Note
            If optimal servo data differ between before and after the workpiece weight setting and newly
            selected servo data is damaged, NC may not be able to launch.
            If the alarm, "4336 Alarm D Reset effective parameter set" appears, reset the NC before turning it
            off. If any of the following alarms results, change the setting back to the previous value.

                1334 Alarm A MCS Servo link data file
                1335 Alarm A Servo data change error

34-3. Parameter Setting and Screens

            WORK WEIGHT SET Screen (1/2)

                                                                                                                                                                                                                  ME32123R0703900050001

            Value in the MAX WORK WEIGHT changes when the optimal servo motor data is updated.

36-1. Overview

            This function allows the in-position check using the in-position value selected by PLC. The NC can

36-2. Parameter Setting

            The system parameter screen has IN-POSITION2 WIDTH and IN-POSITION2 (HOME POS.)
            setting fields. When the second in-position is requested by PLC, it is selected as an in-position
            value. However, the in-position check is not performed if the value is set to "0".
            The indication and setting method are same as IN-POSITION WIDTH and IN-POSITION (HOME
            POS.) though the initial values of IN-POSITION2 WIDTH and IN-POSITION2 (HOME POS.) are "0".
            The function keys "SET" and "ADD" are enabled.
             IN-POSITION2 WIDTH, IN-POSITION2 (HOME POS.)
             Valid range : 0 to 1.000 (unit: 1mm)
             Initial value : 0 (for all axes)

                                                                                                                                6103-E P-269
                                                                                        SECTION 37 MANUAL SKIP FUNCTION

SECTION 37 MANUAL SKIP FUNCTION

37-1. Overview

            When a gauging instrument such as a ring sensor and so on is used, the contact signal between the
            sensor and a workpiece can be input to the NC.
            This function stops the axis travel upon the input of a skip signal (contact signal) to the NC while an
            axis is traveling by manual feed (including pulse handle feed). The function is called the manual skip
            function.
                                                                                   6103-E P-270
                                            SECTION 37 MANUAL SKIP FUNCTION

37-2. Functions

(1) This function is activated in the manual mode and when the manual skip function ON/OFF
      switch on the machine operation panel is turned to ON.

(2) Motions

a. When this function is activated, if the skip signal is turned on during the axis travel by
      manual feed, the axis travel is stopped immediately. The command of feed to the contact
      direction becomes invalid and the command of feed to the direction opposite from contact
      can be valid. The axis travel for both toward and opposite directions is enabled when the
      skip signal is turned off. Only the axis specified with skip signal ON is restricted in travel to
      the contact direction when the skip signal is turned on, other axes can travel regardless of
      skip signal status.

b. If the manual skip function ON/OFF switch is turned off while the skip signal is turned on,
      axis travel prohibition to the contact direction is canceled, enabling travel in both directions.

c. Motion example
      The X-axis is traveling from (A) to (C) by manual feed in the figure below.

                 (A)                (B)             (C)

                                         X

                                         Workpiece

                 Contact direction  ON: Skip signal ON

                                                                    ME32123R0704300020001

 Axis travels from (A) to (C) by manual feed.

 Skip signal turns on at (B)

   At this timing, the feed command becomes invalid to stop the axis travel.

 The X-axis is allowed to travel only in direction to (A) from (B) (in  direction in the

   figure), and travel to (C) is prohibited. Other axes can travel in both directions normally
   as specified by axis feed command.

 If the skip signal is turned off while the X-axis is traveling from (B) to (A), the axis is

   allowed to travel in both directions to (A) and to (C).

           If the manual skip function ON/OFF switch is turned off with the axis at (B) (with the skip
           signal ON), the axis travel command in both directions to (A) and to (C) from (B) is enabled.

(3) Speed clamp of manual skip feed
      Clamping the feed speed is enabled during the manual skip mode. If the feed speed specified
      by axis feed switch or pulse handle exceeds the speed set in the parameter, the speed is
      controlled not to exceed the parameter setting speed by adjusting the speed to become the
      parameter setting value.

Setting parameter : NC optional parameter word No.85

Setting range    : 1 to 10000

Setting unit     : mm/min
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-271
                                                                             SECTION 38 FEED AXIS RETRACT FUNCTION

SECTION 38 FEED AXIS RETRACT FUNCTION

38-1. Overview

            This function allows the tool to be retracted to keep the tool away from the workpiece when the NC
            detects the power failure.

38-2. Function Details

            There are two command methods to enable/disable this function as follows.

               Parameter setting
               Program command

38-2-1.Setting the Parameters on Feed Axis Retract Screen

            Select the parameter setting mode and select the FEED AXIS RETRACT screen using the DISPLAY
            CHANGE key. The function is established by setting DEFAULTS and RETRACT AMOUNT.
            If the retract direction is already specified with the program command, the direction with the program
            command is applied.

       stopped with reset. The temporary stop after started, single block stop, and halt by the program
       stop are regarded as "under the program machining". If the manual interruption mode is

                                                                          6103-E P-273
                        SECTION 38 FEED AXIS RETRACT FUNCTION

38-2-2.Program Command

Activating or deactivating the retract function can be set with the program command. If it is required
to change the retract direction depending on a machining part, ON and OFF function by the program
command is useful.
When the retract command "ON" (G415) is used, the P command value issued together with that is
applied as the retract direction. When the retract command cancel (G414) is issued, the retract
direction follows the value in the parameter.

<< Retract command "ON" >>

          G415 P

                   Set the retract function ON.

                   P : Retract direction specification (0 to 6)

                             0: Not to be retracted
                             1: + X-axis direction
                             2: - X-axis direction
                             3: + Y-axis direction
                             4: - Y-axis direction
                             5: + Z-axis direction
                             6: - Z-axis direction

          If any command other than above is issued or no P command is issued, the following alarm
          appears and the NC stops the machine.
          "Alarm B Feed axis retract function command"

<< Retract command cancel >>
         G414
                  Cancel the G415 command

  G414 (retract command cancel) will be selected when the power is turned on, or when reset.
  This is the modal G code.
  Use this as a special G code, and specify this command individually.
  This G code remains activated during sequence restart searching.
  During the machine lock, G415 is switched to G414.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-274
                                                                             SECTION 38 FEED AXIS RETRACT FUNCTION

38-2-3.When Not Performing Retraction

            The machine is not retracted in the following cases.

            <<Under the program machining>>

               The retract setting is not enabled with the program command or in the parameter. (The retract

                   direction is set to 0, or the retract amount is set to 0.)

               Not during the cutting feed (G01/G02/G03/G132) (Also during the area machining and nose R

                   compensation)

               During the inclined surface machining mode

               During the fixing cycle

               During the manual interruption mode, the slope coordinate and tool axis direction feed input are

                   effective.

               During ATC, APC, and AAC sequence

               The tool is positioned within the area of LIMIT INVALID WIDTH.

            <<Not under the program machining>>

               The retract function other than during the program machining is set to be disabled in the

                   parameter.

               The retract setting is not enabled in the parameter. (The retract direction is set to 0, or the

                   retract amount is set to 0.)

               In the manual operation, the slope coordinate and tool axis direction feed input are effective.

               During ATC, APC, and AAC sequence

               The tool is positioned within the area of LIMIT INVALID WIDTH.

38-2-4.Current Position after Retracted

            The axis moves automatically when retracted with detecting the power failure. Therefore, the axis
            current position differs before and after the power failure by the amount of retracted movement when
            the power is turned on again after the power failure. However, the difference does not always
            become the retract amount set in the parameter.
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-275
             SECTION 39 SPECIAL TOOL LIFE PREDICTION/SPECIAL TOOL LIFE WARNING FUNCTION

SECTION 39 SPECIAL TOOL LIFE PREDICTION/
                   SPECIAL TOOL LIFE WARNING FUNCTION

39-1. Special Tool Life Prediction

            This function activates the status indicators giving a tool life prediction. At the same time, it raises
            alarm D to warn the operator that a tool group is reaching the end of its life.
             The tool life indicators include the status indicator lamp and warning buzzer.

39-1-1.Function

            When there is any group whose total remaining number of tools in the same tool group No. of the
            active tool or magazine mounted tool is reduced to the prediction number (warning point) or less, the
            status indicators are activated and alarm D is raised to give a tool life prediction.
            "4286 Alarm D: Tool life previous notice"

            In the TOOL DATA mode, specify the prediction number (warning point) for each tool group No.
            between 1 and 999 on the "WARNING POINT SET" screen as shown below.

                                                                                                                                                                                           ME32123R0704500020001

                       Fig.39-1 TOOL DATA SETTING: WARNING POINT SET screen

SECTION 39 SPECIAL TOOL LIFE PREDICTION/SPECIAL TOOL LIFE WARNING FUNCTION

[Supplement]

 1) The remaining number of tools in the same tool group No. of the active tool represents the
      remaining value displayed when the management mode has been set at the specified number.

 2) For the tools needing the tool life prediction, set the management mode at the specified
      number. The management mode set at no life control or warning by time is not covered by the
      function.

 3) For the tool group No. set at the specified number and not at the specified number, only the
      tools specified are covered.

 4) For NG tools, the remaining number is considered as zero.
 5) The tools not listed in the "Pot/Tool Correspondence Table" on the MG INFO. sheet are not

      covered by the function.

 6) If the active tool has not been specified or has the group No. of zero, the tool life prediction will
      not be given.

 7) When the tool life is managed in terms of number, the TLFON and TLCO commands are
      necessary.
      Example:
             TLFON
             TLCO Q XXX
      XXX represents a number from 0 to 999 to be counted down.
      If this number is not specified, only one will be counted down. For detail, see SECTION 21
      "Tool Life Management Functions" of Special Functions Manual No. 1.

 8) It is possible to choose to or not to display the alarm "4286 Alarm D: Tool Life Previous Notice"
      using NC optional parameter No. 76 bit 6.
      If it is ON, the tool life prediction alarm will not be displayed. If it is OFF, the tool life prediction
      alarm will be displayed (default).

 9) With the parameter output function, the warning point data can be read and defined with P No.
      "240."
                                                                                                                                6103-E P-277
             SECTION 39 SPECIAL TOOL LIFE PREDICTION/SPECIAL TOOL LIFE WARNING FUNCTION

39-2. Special Tool Life Warning

            This function activates the status indicators to show the tool group has reached the end of life. At the
            same time, it raises an alarm C to warn the operator of the tool life.
             The tool life indicators include the status indicator lamp and warning buzzer.

39-2-1.Function

            When there is any group whose tools are all NG in the same tool group No. of the active tool or
            magazine mounted tool, the status indicators are activated and alarm C is raised to give a tool life
            warning.
            "3277 Alarm C Tool life management no spare tool"
            When above alarm C is raised, change the tool to new one. Set the specified number and remained
            number of the tool on the screen and set OK in OK/NG column.
            If the tool change command is executed for the tool group number where has no spare tool without
            changing the tool to new one, the following alarm B will occur.
            "2436 Alarm B Active tool NG2"

            [Supplement]

              1) All tools in the same tool group No. of the active tool represents tools in all modes.
              2) The tools not listed in the "Pot/Tool Correspondence Table" on the MG INFO. sheet are not

                   covered by the function.
              3) Tool life warning will not be activated when the active tool is not set.
              4) If the group number of active tool is set to 0, the tool life warning is given only for the active

                   tool.
              5) You can set whether displaying the alarm "3277 Alarm C Tool life management no spare tool"

                   or not in the NC optional parameter bit No.76 bit 7.
                   If it is ON, the tool life prediction alarm will not be displayed. If it is OFF, the tool life prediction
                   alarm will be displayed (default).

SECTION 40 VARIABLE LOST MOTION
                   COMPENSATION

40-1. Overview

            The variable lost motion function allows compensation amounts to be changed according to the
            travel distance after a commanded reverse. In addition, a compensation amount is changed
            according to feedrate and interpolation mode (linear/circular).

40-2. Setting Screens

               Select the parameter setting mode.
               Select "BACKLASH COMP./LOST MOTION COMP." by pressing the "DISPLAY CHANGE" key.

                   The following screen appears.

40-3. Function Keys

40-3-1.Axis Switching [F4]

            The axes to be displayed can be changed using the function keys.

40-3-2.Data Transfer [F5]

            (1) The "Data Transfer" key transfers the set data to the range used with the control.
            (2) Pressing this function key shows the popup functions as shown below.

                                                                                                                                                                          ME32123R0704600060001

"1AXIS TRANSFER": The setting values for the axis currently shown are transferred.
"ALL AXIS TRANSFER": All the data for the equipped axes (excluding the spindle and the
machine axis) are transferred.
When the transfer key is pressed, the function keys [F6] (YES) and [F7] (NO) appear.
Make sure to press the emergency button and then press the [F6] (YES) key. If the [F6] (YES)
key is pressed without the emergency button being pressed, the following message appears.

Error 9124 Please push emergency stop. 1

  The compensation amount needs some conditions to be met for the pulse handle travel
      amount on the first page. If not met, the following error message appears.
      - 9121Cannot data transfer. `0<=A<=B1<=B2<=B3<=B4 or 0>=A'
      If A, and B1 to B4 are A>=0, B1, B2, B3, and B4 need to be set with a value to be
      increased from the left.
      If A<=0, specify a value to be decreased.

  The compensation amount to the feedrate on the second page must have the same
      sign. If the condition is not met, the following error message appears.
      - 9121Cannot data transfer. 1 `0<=F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6 or 0>=F'
      Set the same sign to the compensation values of F1 to F6.
                                                                                        6103-E P-283
                       SECTION 40 VARIABLE LOST MOTION COMPENSATION

40-4. System Variable

The compensation amount selections (C1/C2/C3) at circular can be changed using system
variables.

VLMC ( = axis name)
Allowable value 1 to 3 (1=C1 2=C2 3=C3)
Ex.) VLMCX = 1 ... C1 is set as the compensation amount selection with circular of the X-axis.

 These system variables must be specified before starting machining. If attempted during
     machining (in a block where G1, G2 and G3 are issued in series), the program execution
     is stopped when the block before the one to which the variables were specified is
     finished, causing the axis speed to be decelerating.

40-5. Compensation Value Display for the Variable Lost Motion
        Compensation Mode

            Press the operation screen mode key and then press [F7] or [F8] (DISPLAY CHANGE) to display
            the NC axis data. The following items (LOSTMOTION) are shown when the displayed data are
            changed using the cursor key [  ].
            The current compensation amount is displayed for the target axis of the variable lost motion
            compensation mode , and "-----" is displayed to axis of the fixed backlash compensation mode.

40-7. Procedure for Setting Compensation Values

            (1) After the "BACKLASH COMP./LOST MOTION COMP." setting screen is displayed, press the
                   [F4] (AXIS CHANGE) key to display the axis to which the parameters are set.

            (2) Check if the compensation mode is set to "VAR. LOST MOTION". If "FIXED BACKLASH" is
                   being selected, locate the cursor to the compensation mode field and press the [F1] (MENU)
                   key and then the [F3] (VAR.) key to switch the mode to "VAR. LOST MOTION".

                                                                                                                                                                                                                ME32123R0704600100001

            (3) Check if all the setting values are "0.0000" in the compensation amount fields.
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-286
                                                  SECTION 40 VARIABLE LOST MOTION COMPENSATION

(4) Press the page key to display the compensation amount variable function setting screen. At this
      time, check if all the setting values are "0.0000" in the compensation amount fields or the
      compensation amount variable function is set to Disabled.

                                                                                                                                                                                      ME32123R0704600100002

(5) Perform the setups for measurement equipment in the same manner as the conventional
      backlash compensation amount measurement. The following diagram shows an example when
      a Millimess is placed on the spindle side, and a block is on the table side.

   Spindle head  (1)
Magnet stand       (2)

              Millimess                                     ME32123R0704600100003
              Block

Pay attention to the followings when performing the setup.

 Secure the Millimess and the block firmly so that they are not to be moved when the

   spindle speed is increased/decreased by the rapid feedrate.

 Fix the magnet stand and the block without damaging the spindle head and the table.

 Set the magnet stand so that it is not projected too much (the arm is not too long), or not to

   provide unnecessary joint parts.
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-287
                                                  SECTION 40 VARIABLE LOST MOTION COMPENSATION

(6) Measure the reverse characteristics with the pulse handle feed.

       (6)-1. While the Millimess and the block are separated, move the load (table) in the direction
                of (1).

       (6)-2. Keep moving the load (table) in the direction of (1) by the amount of 1 [m] until the
                block contacts the end of the Millimess and then the indication pointer oscillates about
                some tens [m]. While doing this, if the travel command is made in the direction of (2),
                start again from the step (6)-1. as the appropriate result will not be obtained.

       (6)-3. While watching the indication pointer of the Millimess, issue the travel command in the
                direction of (2) using the pulse handle by the amount of 1 [m]. At this time, measure
                the following value.

                  The indication value of the Millimess when the load (table) began to follow the

                       pulse handle command (load moves by 1 [m] when 1 [m] is specified by the
                       pulse handle) ;variation after the command direction is reversed.

      (6)-4. If the value obtained in (6)-3. is 4 [m] or less, set the value of load travel amount L

                to 1 [m] (the initial value). If the value obtained in (6)-3. 4 [m] or more, set a value of

              one fourth of the obtained value as the load travel amount L .

       (6)-5. Keep moving in the direction of (2) by the pulse handle feed, and then retract once.

       (6)-6. Perform the (6)-1. and (6)-2. steps again.

       (6)-7. While watching the indication pointer of the Millimess, issue the travel command in the
                direction of (2) using the pulse handle by the amount of 1 [m]. At this time, measure
                the following value.

                  the pulse amount specified required until the Millimess indicator value changes by

                   1 × L : N1

                  the pulse amount specified required until the Millimess indicator value changes by

                   2 × L : N2

                  the pulse amount specified required until the Millimess indicator value changes by

                   3 × L : N3

                  the pulse amount specified required until the Millimess indicator value changes by

                   4 × L : N4

       (6)-8. Keep moving in the direction of (2) by the pulse handle feed, and then retract again.

       (6)-9. Determine the compensation amount. (See the item 9. for the setting steps for the
                compensation parameter.)

              When the load requires the N1 travel command to move the amount of L , the load
              can move by the amount of L while according to the command if the compensation
              amount B1= (N1 - L ) is added when the travel command for L is issued.
              Other compensation amounts are determined for B2= (N2 - 2 × L ), B3= (N3 - 3 ×

                L ), and B4= (N4 - 4 × L ) in the same manner.

                For the ball screw drive system, set "0.0000" for the reverse compensation amount A.
                Set the compensation amount A only when the pure backlash (mechanical elements in
                looseness or play) is large due to gear connections or similar reasons.
                                                                                                                   6103-E P-288
                                                  SECTION 40 VARIABLE LOST MOTION COMPENSATION

(7) Measure the positioning error by cutting feed / rapid traverse. (This operation is not necessary
      when the compensation amount variable function is not selected.)
       (7)-1. Prepare the NC program to measure positioning errors.

        [Cutting feed]                          [Rapid traverse]

        G90                                     G90
        G0 Z100                                 G0 Z100
        X100                                    X100
        M130                                    M130
        G01 X0 F=VC100                          G0 X0
        G01 Z0 F1000                            G01 Z0 F1000
        M01 ... negative direction positioning  M01 ... negative direction positioning
        G0 Z100                                 G0 Z100
        X-100                                   X-100
        G01 X0 F=VC100                          G0 X0
        G01 Z0 F1000                            G01 Z0 F1000
        M02 ... positive direction positioning  M02 ... positive direction positioning

(7)-2.                                                                                                                                                                                               ME32123R0704600100004
(7)-3.
        Note the indicator value of the Millimess when positioned by the feedrate set to [F2]
        to [F6].

        Set the positioning error by each feedrate as the compensation amount (the indicator
        value difference of the Millimess between positive direction positioning and negative
        direction positioning).

(8) Measure the compensation amount at circular (This operation is not necessary when the
      compensation amount variable function is not selected. When the compensation amount
      variable function is enabled, the compensation amount at the G2/G3 command becomes
      "0.0000" without this setting)
      With circular feed, the compensation amount for reverse needs to be changed depending on
      the feedrate or a size of the circle. This function can select three compensation amounts.
      Obtain the reverse error by a reverse error in actual machining or by the DBB (double ball bar)
      measurement, and determine the compensation amount using the value.
      [Measurement sample]
      DBB measurement

        5m

5m                                If the non-compensation amount is 5 m (200 in.)

                                  when the X-axis and the Y-axis are reversed,

                                  set 0.005 mm (0.00020 in.) as the compensation

                                  amount.

                       5.0 m/div

        Scale

                                                  SECTION 40 VARIABLE LOST MOTION COMPENSATION

(9) Set the load travel amount L determined in (6)-4. and the compensation amount A/B1/B2/B3/

      B4 determined in (6)-9. to [basic function - setting screen] (BACKLASH COMP./ LOST MOTION
      COMP. 1 page), and the compensation amount determined in (7)-3.and (8) to [compensation
      amount variable function - setting screen] (BACKLASH COMP./ LOST MOTION COMP. 2
      page). Locate the cursor to the parameter setting field to be updated, press the [F1] (SET) key,
      and then input the values. The unit of the value to be set follows "SET UNIT" shown on the right
      corner of the screen.
(10) After all the parameters has been set, check if the emergency status is being selected. Then
      press the [F5] (DATA TRANSFER) key. At this time, to enable the setting parameters of the axis

      parameters of all the axes including the axes not shown on the screen, select the [F3] (ALL
      AXES TRANSFER) key.

                                                                                                                                                                                      ME32123R0704600100007

      parameters are enabled.
                                                                                                                    6103-E P-290
                                                   SECTION 40 VARIABLE LOST MOTION COMPENSATION

 (12) Check the compensation effect. Check if the compensation is insufficient or excessive, and if it
       is, correct the compensation value. After the compensation value is corrected, perform the
       steps 10. and 11., and enable the set parameter.

Checking the measurement result (circular)

                    Check if the compensation amount is working properly.
                    When performing actual machining, measure the steps
                    generated at reverse using the measurement result of
                    a workpiece (circularity value, etc.) to determine the
                    compensation amount.

             Scale

      (DISPLAY CHANGE) to display the NC-axis data. The current compensation amount is shown

      the compensation mode.

SECTION 41 USER MAINTENANCE FUNCTION

41-1. Overview

            This function allows users to set maintenance items as they need.

41-2. Function Detail

            Select the USER MAINTENANCE screen from the PARAMETER screen.
            Set the data for the user regular maintenance parameters from No.1 to No.24.
            If a counted hour exceeds the notification hour and warning hour, an alarm D and alarm C are raised
            respectively.

41-2-1.Parameter Setting on the User Maintenance Screen

  Name                                                                                                                                      ME32123R0704700030001

 WARN.   NOTI.
         An alarm C appears when a counted hour (COUNT) reaches a setting value of
 COUNT   WARN.
  TYPE   The counted hour (COUNT) from the day when maintenance is performed.
CHANGE   Type of hour counting.
         Date (year/month/day) when maintenance is performed.

                                          SECTION 41 USER MAINTENANCE FUNCTION

[COUNT]

   The default is "0". The maximum value is "999999".

   Setting is disabled.

   The unit is hour (COUNT). When no TYPE is selected, the characters in the row are displayed

      in pale blue.

   When ALL TIME is selected for TYPE, the counted hour is counted from the day when

      maintenance is performed at the power ON.
      When other types are selected, it is started with the last value. (When no TYPE is selected, it is
      started with "0".)

   When CHANGE is "0/ 0/ 0", the counted hour is not accumulated regardless of the selected

      TYPE.
      The counted hour is also not accumulated when no TYPE is selected.

[TYPE]

 No type is selected at default.

 Changing the TYPE loads the default value "0/ 0 /0" at CHANGE, which also clears the counted

   hour to "0".

   Mode end               Disabled

 During mode

                    SECTION 41 USER MAINTENANCE FUNCTION

41-2-2.CHANGE MODE

To change the ITEM and TYPE on the User Maintenance screen, it is necessary to switch the mode
between "change mode" and "end mode change" modes by pressing [F1] (CHANGE MODE) which
is shown by pressing the extension function key. (Note that the ITEM cannot be changed when a
user maintenance file is provided as described in "Parameters detail".)

   By pressing [F1] (CHANGE MODE) during the END MODE CHANGE mode, the message

      "Change mode OK?" appears as shown in the screen below. Select YES to select the "change
      mode" mode.

   By pressing [F1] (END MODE CHANGE) during the "change mode" mode, the mode is

      terminated.

   The data set in the "change mode" mode are backed up.

During the "mode change" mode, the indication CHANGE MODE appears on the upper right side.

                                                                             SECTION 41 USER MAINTENANCE FUNCTION

41-2-3.User Maintenance Definition File

            It is possible to set the data of ITEM on the User Maintenance screen by using a text file.
            This file is called "user maintenance definition file", which is stored at and with the name as: "D:/
            MD1/USERMAINTE/USERMAINTE.TXT".
            In each line of the file, prepare the data as follows. (Up to 250-byte characters per line can be written
            and the file size should be within 10 Kbyte.)

           <<NO. of ITEM>>, <<message for ITEM>>, <<comment>>

               <<NO. of ITEM>>

                   Nos. from 1 to 24
                   If nos. or characters (including space, TAB) other than 1 to 24, the line is ignored.
                   If the same nos. exist, the first one is applied.

               <<Message for ITEM>>

                   Message up to 16 byte characters
                   The characters over 16 bytes are ignored. Thus a 2-byte character used after 15 bytes may be
                   shown half.
                   This item is not covered by multi-language. The message will not be displayed properly if the
                   language is switched.
                   When no message is input (as shown in "1,,"), no message is shown.

               <<Comment>>

                   Not used.

            In addition, if <<NO. of ITEM>> and the first "," are given in succession from the top of the line, and
            a no.
            between 1 and 24 is selected for <<NO. of ITEM>>, the ITEM is regarded as defined.

Example:                                                              The ITEM for No.11 is "ITEM-A".

    11,ITEM-A,***                                                     (Ignored since a space exists in front of <<NO. of
      2,ITEM-B,***                                                        ITEM>>)

    25,ITEM-B,***                                                     (Ignored since the item no. is out of the range.)
    2,
    2,1234567890123456,***                                            No message for the ITEM of No.2.
    3,user.period.nameA,***                                               (Ignored since the No.2 has been registered already.)

                                              ME32123R0704700050001   The ITEM for No.3 is "user.period.name"
                                                                          ("A" is ignored since it exceeds 16 bytes.)

                                                                      (Other items are shown in blank since they are not
                                                                          defined.)
                                                                          (The TYPE is not selected.)
